    quote: "{\n  \"clause_id\": \"3.0\",\n  \"fragments\": [\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 12,\n      \"text\": \"\
      3.0      Compliance\\n\\n3.1     Memorandum of Understanding (MOU)\\n\\n\\n3.1.1   A Memorandum of Understanding\
      \ placed at Appendix No.: 1 shall be executed after\\n          the award of contract before commencement\
      \ of work by the contractor with regard to\\n          various provisions on Safety, Health and Environment\
      \ to be practiced during the\\n          construction work.\\n\\n\\n3.2     BMRC’s SHE Policy and Management\
      \ Systems\\n\\n\\n3.2.1    The construction works shall be undertaken in accordance with BMRC’s SHE Policy\\\
      n        and Management Systems as amended from time to time provided in Project SHE\\n         Manual.\\\
      n\\n\\n3.3      Indian statutory requirements\\n\\n3.3.1     Primary statutory regulations\\n\\n\\n3.3.1.1\
      \  Contractor shall develop thorough understanding about Building and Other\\n        Construction Workers\
      \ (Regulation of Employment and Conditions of Service) Act\\n        1996, Central Rules 1998, the building\
      \ & other construction workers welfare cess\\n        act 1996 and  central welfare rules 1998, Building and\
      \ Other Construction\\n       Workers [RE&C] Karnataka  Rules,  2006,  Building and Other Construction\\n\
      \       Workers Welfare Cess act 1996 and Central Rules 1998, Notification [Central &\\n         State] –\
      \ Collection of Cess, not only to satisfy the Inspectors’ perspective but the\\n       use  of  legislation\
      \  as  the  strong  tool  for  effective SHE management  at\\n        construction worksites. Contractor is\
      \ strongly advised to practice the principle\\n         of voluntary compliance.\\n\\n\\n 3.3.1.2 In order\
      \ to facilitate the contractor for better understanding on the various provisions of\\n         the above\
      \ Act and Rules, a tabulated information highlighting the Sections/Rules\\n          referring to the corresponding\
      \ registration of contractors, maintenance of registers and\\n         records, hours of work and wages, cess\
      \ & welfare, medical  facilities and safety\\n        requirements are given in Appendix No. 2. It is an indicative\
      \ one and not a limiting list.\\n\\n\\n3.3.2   In addition, the construction works shall be undertaken in\
      \ accordance with all applicable\\n         legislation and Indian statutory requirements listed below but\
      \ not limiting to:\\n\\n\\n                i)     Indian Electricity Act 2003 and Rules 1956\\n\\n       \
      \           ii)    National Building Code, 2005\\n\\n                  iii)    Factories Act, 1948, Delhi,\
      \ Uttarpradesh, Haryana Factories Rules.\\n\\n             iv)   Motor Vehicles Act as amended in 1994 and\
      \ The Central Motor Vehicles Rules,\\n              1989.\\n\\n           v)    Indian Road Congress Code\
      \ IRC: SP: 55-2001 ‘Guidelines on Safety In Road\\n               Construction Zones.\\n\\n             vi)\
      \   The Petroleum Act, 1934 and Rules 1976\\n\\n              vii)  Gas Cylinder Rules, 2003\\n\\n       \
      \        viii)   Indian Explosives Act. 1884, along with the Explosives substance Act 1908 and\\n        \
      \        the Explosives Rules 1983\\n\\n             ix)   The (Indian) Boilers Act, 1923\\n\\n          \
      \ x)   The Public Liability Insurance Act 1991 and Rules 1991\\n\\n             xi)   Minimum Wages Act, 1948\
      \ and Rules 1950\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 13,\n      \"text\": \"xii)   Contract Labour Act,\
      \ 1970 and Rules 1971\\n\\n                               xiii)   Child Labour (Prohibitions & Regulations)\
      \ Act, 1986 and Rules 1950\\n\\n                        xiv)  Environment Protection Act, 1986 and Rules 1986\\\
      n\\n                     xv)   Air (Prevention and control of Pollution) Act, 1981\\n\\n                 \
      \       xvi)  Water (Prevention and Control of Pollution) Act, 1974\\n\\n                          xvii) \
      \ The Noise Pollution (Regulation & Control) Rules, 2000\\n\\n                           xviii)  Notification\
      \ on Control of Noise from Diesel Generator (DG) sets, 2002\\n\\n                       xix)  Recycled Plastic\
      \ Usage Rules, 1998\\n\\n                    xx)   Notification, Central Ground Water Board, Act January 1997\\\
      n\\n                       xxi)  Manufacture, Storage & Import of Hazardous Chemicals Rules, 1989\\n\\n  \
      \                        xxii)  The Hazardous Waste (Management & Handling) Rules, 1989\\n\\n            \
      \               xxiii) Hazardous Waste Management Rules 1989 (as amended in 1999)\\n\\n                  \
      \    xxiv) Karnataka Preservation of Trees act, 1976 & rules, Batteries (Management and\\n               \
      \          Handling) Rules\\n\\n                    xxv)  Fly ash utilization notification, Sept 1999 as amended\
      \ in August 2003\\n\\n                      xxvi) Guidelines of Karnataka Urban Arts Commission\\n\\n    \
      \                    xxvii) Mysore Tramway Act.\\n\\n\\n          3.3.3    Workman Compensation Act, 1923\
      \ along with allied Rules\\n\\n\\n           3.3.3.1  The contractor shall ensure that all his employees /\
      \ workmen are covered under\\n               ‘Workmen Compensation Act’ and shall pay compensation to his\
      \ workmen as\\n               and when the eventuality for the same arises.\\n\\n\\n          3.3.4     Notwithstanding\
      \ the above Act/Rules, there  is nothing  in those  to exempt the\\n                     contractor from the\
      \ purview of any other Act or Rule in Republic of India for the safety\\n                      of men and\
      \ materials.\\n\\n\\n          3.3.5        If the requirements stated in this document are less stringent\
      \ than or in conflict with\\n                    the country’s applicable legislation, the latter shall apply.\\\
      n\\n\\n          3.4       International Standards, Guidelines & ISO Certifications\\n\\n\\n          3.4.1\
      \    The works should be undertaken in accordance with the applicable international\\n                   \
      \  guidelines, standards and specifications on SHE and every contract shall aim to\\n                   achieve\
      \ ISO certifications listed below during the currency of the contract:\\n\\n\\n             OHSAS 18001-1999\
      \   : Occupational Health and Safety Management System.\\n\\n                ISO 14001-2004         : Environmental\
      \ Management Systems.\\n\\n          3.4.2    The process of certification shall start immediately after the\
      \ award of the work and\\n                  complete within reasonable time. Towards this, the contractor\
      \ shall undertake the\\n                    required steps including appointment of ISO consultant for obtaining\
      \ the certification\\n                on  Occupational  Health  and  Safety  Management  System  and  Environment\\\
      n                Management System.\\n\\n\\n          3.4.3     In case of failure on the part of the contractor,\
      \ the Employer at the cost of the\\n                  contractor shall do the same.\"\n    }\n  ],\n  \"heading_line\"\

  text: Contractor obligations under Vol 3 Part 2 encompass mandatory statutory notifications, deployment of certified
    safety personnel, adherence to standard codes of practice (BIS, IRC, BOCWR), and establishment of formal permit-to-work
    and traffic management systems.

      \    0.0,\n    0.0,\n    595.219970703125,\n    842.0\n  ],\n  \"pdf_page\": 56,\n  \"review_status\": \"\
      pending_manual_review\",\n  \"rotation\": 0,\n  \"text\": \"Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\\n  \
      \                                                        240\\n                i)    every compressed air\
      \ system in a tunnel is provided with emergency power\\n               supply for maintained continued supply\
      \ of compressed air as per Rule 155 of\\n         BOCWR\\n\\n                  ii)    watertight bulkhead\
      \ doors are installed at the entrance of a tunnel to prevent\\n                 flooding.\\n\\n          \
      \        iii)    reliable and effective means of communication such as telephone or walkie-\\n           \
      \        talkie are provided and maintained for arranging better effective communication\\n              \
      \   at an excavation or tunnelling work as per Rule 136 of BOCWR.\\n\\n             iv)     all portable electrical\
      \ hand tools and inspection lamp used in under ground and\\n               confined space at an excavation\
      \ or tunnelling work is operated at a voltage not\\n              exceeding 24V.\\n\\n           v)    only\
      \ flame proof equipment of appropriate type as per IS:5571:2000 and or\\n                other relevant national\
      \ standard is used inside the tunnel\\n\\n             vi)    petrol or LPG of any other flammable substances\
      \ are not used, stored inside the\\n                tunnel except with prior approval from Employer, and also\
      \ no oxy-acetylene gas\\n                    is used in a compressed air environment in excavation or tunnelling\\\
      n\\n              vii)   adequate number of water outlets provided for fire fighting purpose, an audible\\\
      n                     fire alarm and adequate number and types of fire extinguishers are provided\\n     \
      \        and maintained.\\n\\n               viii)  temperature in any working chamber in an excavation or\
      \ tunnelling work where\\n              workers employed does not exceed 29C as per Rule 165 of BOCWR.\\\
      n\\n             ix)     all working areas in a free air tunnel are provided with ventilation system as\\\
      n              approved by the Director General and the fresh air supplied in such tunnel is not\\n      \
      \          less than 6 m3/ min for each worker employed in tunnel as per Rule 153 of\\n          BOCWR.\\\
      n\\n\\n34.3     Warning signs and notices:\\n\\n\\n34.3.1   The contractor shall ensure that\\n\\n       \
      \         i)     suitable warning signs or notices, required for the safety of building workers\\n       \
      \         carrying out the work of an excavation or tunnelling, shall be displayed or\\n               erected\
      \ at conspicuous  places in Hindi and in a language understood by\\n                majority of such building\
      \ workers at such building such excavation or tunnelling\\n              work\\n\\n                  ii) \
      \   such warning signs and notices with regard to compressed air working shall\\n                include\\\
      n\\n                a)    the danger involved in such compressed air work\\n\\n                b)     fire\
      \ and explosion hazard\\n\\n                 c)    the emergency procedures for rescue from such danger or\
      \ hazards.\\n\\n\\n35.0    Work Permit system\\n\\n\\n35.1    The Contractor shall develop a Work Permit system,\
      \ which is a formal written system\\n        used to control certain types of work that are potentially hazardous.\
      \ A work permit is a\\n         document, which specifies the work to be done, and the precautions to be taken.\
      \ Work\\n         Permits form an essential part of safe systems of work for many construction activities.\\\
      n        They allow work to start only after safe procedures have been defined and they\\n          provide\
      \ a clear record that all foreseeable hazards have been considered. Permits to\\n        Work are usually\
      \ required in high-risk areas as identified by the Risk Assessments.\\n\\n\\n35.2    A permit  is needed when\
      \ construction work can only be carried out   if normal\\n         safeguards are dropped or when new hazards\
      \ are introduced by the work. Examples\\n           of high-risk activities include but are not limited to:\\\
      n\\n\\n                 i)     Entry into confined spaces\\n                  ii)   Work in close proximity\
      \ to overhead power lines and telecommunication cables.\\n                  iii)   Hot work.\\n          \
      \   iv)   To dig—where underground services may be located.\\n           v)   Work with heavy moving machinery.\\\
      n\\n\\nBangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.              September 2026     Section Page 52 of 114\",\n \

      : [\n        82.43972778320312,\n        38.09945297241211,\n        512.7691650390625,\n        790.1315307617188\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 3,\n      \"text\": \"i) \\nevery compressed air system in a tunnel is\
      \ provided with emergency power \\nsupply for maintained continued supply of compressed air as per Rule 155\
      \ of \\nBOCWR \\nii) \\nwatertight bulkhead doors are installed at the entrance of a tunnel to prevent \\\
      nflooding. \\niii) \\nreliable and effective means of communication such as telephone or walkie-\\ntalkie\
      \ are provided and maintained for arranging better effective communication \\nat an excavation or tunnelling\
      \ work as per Rule 136 of BOCWR. \\niv) \\nall portable electrical hand tools and inspection lamp used in\
      \ under ground and \\nconfined space at an excavation or tunnelling work is operated at a voltage not \\nexceeding\
      \ 24V. \\nv) \\nonly flame proof equipment of appropriate type as per IS:5571:2000 and or \\nother relevant\
      \ national standard is used inside the tunnel \\nvi) \\npetrol or LPG of any other flammable substances are\
      \ not used, stored inside the \\ntunnel except with prior approval from Employer, and also no oxy-acetylene\
      \ gas \\nis used in a compressed air environment in excavation or tunnelling \\nvii) \\nadequate number of\
      \ water outlets provided for fire fighting purpose, an audible \\nfire alarm and adequate number and types\
      \ of fire extinguishers are provided \\nand maintained. \\nviii) \\ntemperature in any working chamber in\
      \ an excavation or tunnelling work where \\nworkers employed does not exceed 29C as per Rule 165 of BOCWR.\
      \ \\nix) \\nall working areas in a free air tunnel are provided with ventilation system as \\napproved by\
      \ the Director General and the fresh air supplied in such tunnel is not \\nless than 6 m3/ min for each worker\
      \ employed in tunnel as per Rule 153 of \\nBOCWR. \\n \\n34.3 \\nWarning signs and notices: \\n \\n34.3.1\
      \ \\nThe contractor shall ensure that  \\ni) \\nsuitable warning signs or notices, required for the safety\
      \ of building workers \\ncarrying out the work of an excavation or tunnelling, shall be displayed or \\nerected\
      \ at conspicuous  places in Hindi and in a language understood by \\nmajority of such building workers at\
      \ such building such excavation or tunnelling \\nwork \\nii) \\nsuch warning signs and notices with regard\
      \ to compressed air working shall \\ninclude \\na) \\nthe danger involved in such compressed air work \\nb)\
      \ \\nfire and explosion hazard \\nc) \\nthe emergency procedures for rescue from such danger or hazards. \\\
      n \\n35.0 \\nWork Permit system \\n \\n35.1 \\nThe Contractor shall develop a Work Permit system, which is\
      \ a formal written system \\nused to control certain types of work that are potentially hazardous. A work\
      \ permit is a \\ndocument, which specifies the work to be done, and the precautions to be taken. Work \\nPermits\
      \ form an essential part of safe systems of work for many construction activities. \\nThey allow work to start\
      \ only after safe procedures have been defined and they \\nprovide a clear record that all foreseeable hazards\
      \ have been considered. Permits to \\nWork are usually required in high-risk areas as identified by the Risk\
      \ Assessments. \\n \\n35.2  \\nA permit is needed when construction work can only be carried out if normal\
      \ \\nsafeguards are dropped or when new hazards are introduced by the work. Examples \\nof high-risk activities\
      \ include but are not limited to: \\n \\ni) \\nEntry into confined spaces \\nii) \\nWork in close proximity\
      \ to overhead power lines and telecommunication cables. \\niii) \\nHot work. \\niv) \\nTo dig—where underground\
      \ services may be located. \\nv) \\nWork with heavy moving machinery. \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\"\
      : [\n        546.0439453125,\n        24.479991912841797,\n        570.0199584960938,\n        41.38799285888672\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 4,\n      \"text\": \"240\\n\"\n    }\n  ]\n}"

      pending_manual_review\",\n  \"rotation\": 0,\n  \"text\": \"Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\\n241\\\
      n                          vi)   Working on electrical equipment\\n                             vii)  Work\
      \ with radioactive isotopes.\\n                               viii)  Heavy lifting operations and lifting\
      \ operations closer to live power line\\n\\n\\n          35.3    The permit-to-work system should be fully\
      \ documented, laying down:\\n\\n\\n                                 i)   How the system works;\\n        \
      \                            ii)   The jobs it is to be used for;\\n                                     iii)\
      \   The responsibilities and training of those involved; and\\n                          iv)  How to check\
      \ its operation;\\n\\n\\n          35.4    A Work Permit authorisation form shall be completed with the maximum\
      \ duration\\n                    period not exceeding 12 hours.\\n\\n\\n          35.5    A copy of each Permit\
      \ To Work shall be displayed, during its validity, in a conspicuous\\n                     location in close\
      \ proximity to the actual works location to which it applies.\\n\\n\\n          36.0      Traffic Management\\\
      n\\n\\n          36.1    The basic objective of the following guidelines  is to lay down procedures to be\\\
      n                  adopted by contractor to ensure the safe and efficient movement of traffic and also to\\\
      n                  ensure the safety of workmen at construction sites.\\n\\n\\n          36.2       All construction\
      \ workers should be provided with high visibility jackets with reflective\\n                   tapes as most\
      \ of viaduct /tunnelling/station and depot works or either above or under\\n                      right-of-way.\
      \ The conspicuity of workmen at  all times shall be increased so as to\\n                     protect from\
      \ speeding vehicular traffic.\\n\\n\\n          36.3    The guiding principles to be adopted for safety in\
      \ construction zone are to\\n\\n\\n                                 i)    Warn the road user clearly and sufficiently\
      \ in advance.\\n\\n                                    ii)    Provide safe and clearly marked lanes for guiding\
      \ road users.\\n\\n                                     iii)    Provide safe and clearly marked buffer and\
      \ work zones\\n\\n                          iv)   Provide adequate measures that control driver behaviour\
      \ through construction\\n                        zones.\\n\\n\\n          36.4     Legal permission\\n\\n\\\
      n          36.4.1    In  all cases, the contractor shall employ proper precautions. Wherever operations\\\
      n                  undertaken are likely to interfere with public traffic, specific traffic management plans\\\
      n                      shall be drawn up and implemented by the contractor in consultation with the approval\\\
      n                      of local police authorities and/or the concerned metropolitan/civil authorities as\
      \ the\\n                 case may be.\\n\\n\\n          36.4.2   Such  traffic management plans  shall  include\
      \  provision  for  traffic  diversion and\\n                     selection of alternative routes for transport\
      \ of equipment.  If necessary, the contractor\\n                      shall carry out road widening before\
      \ commencement of works to accommodate the\\n                    extra load\\n\\n\\n          36.5    The\
      \ primary traffic control devices used in work zones shall include signs, delineators,\\n                \
      \    barricades, cones, pylons, pavement markings and flashing lights.\\n\\n\\n          36.6    The road\
      \ construction and maintenance signs which fall into the same three major\\n                    categories\
      \ as do other traffic signs, that are Regulatory Signs, Warning Signs and\\n                     Direction\
      \ (or guidelines) Signs shall only be used. The IRC: 67 (Code of Practice for\\n               Road Signs)\
      \ provide a list of traffic signs. The size, colours and placement of sign\\n                      shall confirm\
      \ to IRC: 67.\\n\\n\\n\\n         Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.              September 2026     Section\
      \ Page 53 of 114\",\n  \"text_blocks\": [\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        50.15998077392578,\n        15.768376350402832,\n\

      n                          vi)    debris / bricks and other materials or articles shall be removed by means\
      \ of\\n                           a)      chutes\\n                           b)     buckets or hoists\\n\
      \                            c)      through openings through floors or\\n                           d)  \
      \   any other safe means\\n\\n                             vii)   no person other than  building workers \
      \ or other persons  essential  to the\\n                         operation of demolition work shall be permitted\
      \ to enter a zone of demolition and\\n                          the area be provided with substantial barricades.\\\
      n\\n\\n          34.0     Excavation and Tunnelling:\\n\\n\\n          34.1     Excavation\\n\\n\\n      \
      \    34.1.1   The contractor shall ensure\\n\\n\\n                                 i)    where any construction\
      \ building worker engaged in excavation is exposed to\\n                       hazard of falling or sliding\
      \ material or article from any bank or side of such\\n                         excavation which is more than\
      \ one 1.5 m above his footing, such worker is\\n                          protected by adequate piling and\
      \ bracing against such bank or side.\\n\\n\\n                                    ii)    where banks of an\
      \ excavation are undercut, adequate shoring is provided to\\n                         support the material\
      \ or article overhanging such bank.\\n\\n\\n                                     iii)   excavated material\
      \ is not stored at least 0.65 m from the edge of an open\\n                         excavation or trench and\
      \ banks of such excavation or trench are stripped of\\n                         loose rocks and the banks\
      \ of such excavation or trench are stripped of loose\\n                         rocks and other materials\
      \ which may slide,  roll or  fall upon a construction\\n                           building worker working\
      \ below such bank\\n\\n\\n                          iv)   metal ladders and staircases or ramps are provided,\
      \ as the case may be, for\\n                         safe access to and egress from excavation where, the\
      \ depth of such excavation\\n                      exceeds 1.5 m and such ladders, staircases or ramps comply\
      \ with the IS 3696\\n                          Part 1&2 and other relevant national standards.\\n\\n\\n  \
      \                    v)    trench and excavation  is protected against  falling of a person by suitable\\\
      n                     measures  if the depth of such trench or excavation exceeds 1.5 m and such\\n      \
      \                     protection is an improved protection in accordance with the design and drawing\\n  \
      \                          of a professional engineer, where such depth exceeds 4m.\\n\\n\\n          34.2\
      \      Tunnelling\\n\\n\\n          34.2.1   The contractor shall inform in writing to the Director General\
      \ within 30 days, prior to\\n                    the commencement of any tunnelling work.\\n\\n\\n       \
      \   34.2.2   The contractor shall appoint a responsible person for safe operation for tunnelling\\n      \
      \           work as per Rule 121 & 125 of BOCWR.\\n\\n\\n          34.2.3   The contractor shall ensure\\\

- kind: summary
  text: The contractor bears all costs for required employee medical examinations, maintains confidential records,
    and ensures compliance with occupational health, welfare, and noise control standards.

  - id: J44c81c9cdd51865e
    okf_path: OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3.md
    okf_sha256: b973183de77a23a5c4e6f7df14a6bfe6e19a3add3e80a6dc1db238991aff8dd3
    heading: Clause 42.0 — Medical Facilities
    source_document: Vol3DLNP1.pdf
    source_sha256: 1ee3fc8dfd12f659f34446b0a6db74e1e1cdd4d593ca91b2a48d59bf1ef97d52
    pdf_pages:

    json_path: parsed_json/vol3/part_3_health_welfare/health_welfare_clauses.json
    json_sha256: dc172436c564fe7d5a449f7f786e6863b16e68ebd59020d555002ab6fc04758d
    json_pointer: /clauses/1
    json_record_id: null
    source_review_status: pending_manual_review
    quote: "{\n  \"clause_id\": \"42.0\",\n  \"fragments\": [\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 64,\n      \"text\": \"\
      42.0     Medical Facilities\\n\\n\\n42.1     Medical Examination\\n\\n\\n42.1.1   The contractor shall arrange\
      \ a medical examination of all his employees including his\\n          sub-contractor employees employed as\
      \ drivers, operators of  lifting appliances and\\n          transport equipment before employing, after illness\
      \ or injury,  if  it appears that the\\n           illness or injury might have affected his fitness and,\
      \ thereafter, once in every two years\\n        up to the age of 40 and once in a year, thereafter.\\n\\n\\\
      n                i)    The Contractor shall maintain the confidential records of medical examination or\\\
      n                the physician authorized by the Employer.\\n\\n                  ii)   No building or other\
      \ construction worker is charged for the medical examination\\n             and the cost of such examination\
      \ is borne by contractor employing such building\\n               worker.\\n\\n                  iii)   The\
      \ medical examination shall include: -\\n\\n\\n                a)     Full medical and occupational history.\\\
      n\\n                b)     Clinical examination with particular reference to\\n\\n                       \
      \             i)    General Physique;\\n\\n                                       ii)     Vision:- Total visual\
      \ performance using standard orthorator  like\\n                         Titmus  Vision  Tester  should be\
      \  estimated and  suitability  for\\n                         placement  ascertained  in accordance  with\
      \  the  prescribed  job\\n                           standards.\\n\\n                                    \
      \    iii)    Hearing:  - Persons with normal must be able to hear a forced\\n                          whisper\
      \ at twenty-four feet. Persons using hearing aids must be\\n                           able to hear a warning\
      \ shout under noisy working conditions.\\n\\n                             iv)    Breathing:- Peak flow rate\
      \ using standard peak flow meter and the\\n                        average peak flow rate determined out of\
      \ these readings of the test\\n                          performed.  The  results  recorded  at  pre-placement\
      \  medical\\n                          examination could be used as a standard for the same individual at\\\
      n                           the same altitude for reference during subsequent examination.\\n\\n         \
      \               v)   Upper Limbs: - Adequate arm function and grip\\n\\n                             vi) \
      \   Spine: - Adequately flexible for the job concerned.\\n\\n                               vii)   Lower Limbs:\
      \ - Adequate leg and foot concerned.\\n\\n                                 viii)   General: - Mental alertness\
      \ and stability with good eye, hand and\\n                              foot coordination.\\n\\n         \
      \        c)   Any other tests which the examining doctor considers necessary\\n\\n\\n42.1.2.     If the contractor\
      \ fails to get the medical examination conducted as mentioned above,\\n          the employer will have the\
      \ right to get the same conducted by through an agency with\\n           intimation to the contractor and\
      \ deduct the cost and overhead charges.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 65,\n      \"text\": \"42.2\
      \     Occupational Health Centre\\n\\n          42.2.1   The contractor shall ensure at a construction site\
      \ an occupational health centre,\\n                 mobile or static is provided and maintained in good order.\
      \ Services and facilities\\n                as per the scale lay down in Schedule X of BOCWR. A construction\
      \ medical\\n                     officer appointed in an occupational health centre, possess the qualification\
      \ as\\n                     laid down in Schedule XI of BOCWR.\\n\\n\\n          42.3    Ambulance van and\
      \ room\\n\\n\\n          42.3.1   The contractor  shall ensure  at a construction  site of a building or other\\\
      n                  construction work that an ambulance van and room are provided at such\\n              \
      \    construction site or an arrangement is made with a nearby hospital for providing\\n                such\
      \ ambulance van for transportation of serious cases of accident or sickness\\n                   of workers\
      \  to  hospital promptly and such ambulance van and room are\\n                 maintained in good repair\
      \ and is equipped with standard facilities specified in\\n                Schedule IV and Schedule V of BOCWR.\\\
      n\\n\\n          42.4      First-aid boxes\\n\\n\\n          42.4.1   The contractor shall ensure at a construction\
      \ site one First-aid box for 100 workers\\n                   provided and maintained for providing First-aid\
      \ to the building workers. Every First-aid\\n                 box  is  distinctly marked  “First-aid” and\
      \  is equipped with the  articles specified  in\\n                 Schedule III of BOCWR.\\n\\n\\n       \
      \   42.5     HIV/ AIDS prevention and control\\n\\n\\n          42.5.1   The contractor shall adopt the Employer’s\
      \ Policy on “HIV  / AIDS Prevention and\\n                    Control for Workmen Engaged by Contractors”\
      \ and the copy of the policy is given in\\n                Appendix No.: 4.\\n\\n          42.5.2   The Employer\
      \ will engage a professional agency for implementing the guidelines laid\\n               down in the policy\
      \ and communicate to the contractor.\\n\\n          42.5.3   The Contractor shall extend necessary support\
      \ to the appointed agency by deputing\\n                    the workmen to attend the awareness creation programmes.\\\
      n\\n          42.5.4   The contractor shall also extend necessary organizational support to the appointed\\\
      n                 agency  for the  effective implementation  of the Employers’ workplace  policy on\\n   \
      \              HIV/AIDS for workmen of the Contractors.\\n\\n          42.5.5   As laid down in the policy\
      \ the contractor shall identify peer educators (1 for every 100\\n                   workers) and refer them\
      \ for professional training to the Employers’ appointed agency\\n                       for the purpose.\\\
      n\\n          42.5.6   The peer educators on completion of the training shall serve as the focal point\\n\
      \                    for any information, education and awareness campaign among the workmen\\n          \
      \       throughout the contract period.\\n\\n          42.5.7   The peer educators will be paid a monthly\
      \ honorarium as fixed by the Employer for\\n                   rendering his services in addition to his regular\
      \ duty.\\n\\n          42.5.8   The total number of peer educators (1 for 100 workers) shall always be maintained\
      \ by\\n                    the contractor.\\n\\n          42.5.9    In case if these peer educators leave\
      \ the contractor by creating vacancy, then\\n                  the contractor at his own expense train the\
      \ new replacement peer educator from\\n                  the Employers’ appointed agency for the purpose.\\\
      n          42.5.10    It is suggested to the contractor that due care should be taken to select the peer\\\
      n                   educators from among the group of workmen so that they remain with the contractor\\n \
      \                  throughout the contract period.\\n\\n          42.6     Prevention of mosquito breeding\"\
      \n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"text\": \"42.6.1   Measures shall be taken to prevent mosquito\
      \ breeding at site. The measures to be\\n         taken shall include:\\n\\n\\n                i)    Empty\
      \ cans, oil drums, packing and other receptacles, which may retain water\\n                  shall be deposited\
      \ at a central collection point and shall be removed from the\\n                   site regularly.\\n\\n \
      \                 ii)      Still waters shall be treated at least once every week with oil in order to prevent\\\
      n              mosquito breeding.\\n\\n                  iii)    Contractor’s equipment and other items on\
      \ the site, which may retain water,\\n                  shall be stored, covered or treated in such a manner\
      \ that water could not be\\n                 retained.\\n\\n             iv)   Water storage tanks shall be\
      \ provided.\\n\\n\\n42.6.2    Posters in both Hindi, English and local language which draw attention to the\
      \ dangers\\n           of permitting mosquito breeding, shall be displayed prominently on the site.\\n\\n\\\
      n42.6.3   The contractor at periodic interval shall arrange to prevent mosquito breeding\\n        by fumigation\
      \  / spraying of insecticides. Most  effective insecticides  shall\\n         include SOLFAC WP 10 or Baytex,\
      \ The Ideal Larvicide etc.\\n\\n\\n42.7     Alcohol and drugs\\n\\n\\n42.7.1   The contractor shall ensure\
      \ at  all times that no employee  is working under the\\n          influence of alcohol / drugs which are\
      \ punishable under Govt. regulations.\\n\\n\\n42.7.2   Smoking  at  public worksites by any employee  is also\
      \ prohibited as per Govt.\\n          regulations.\"\n    }\n  ],\n  \"heading_line\": \"42.0     Medical\

      source_line_number\": 64,\n      \"text\": \"          42.5.9    In case if these peer educators leave the\
      \ contractor by creating vacancy, then\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 65,\n      \"source_line_number\"\
      : 65,\n      \"text\": \"                  the contractor at his own expense train the new replacement peer\
      \ educator from\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 65,\n      \"source_line_number\": 66,\n      \"text\"\
      : \"                  the Employers’ appointed agency for the purpose.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\"\
      : 65,\n      \"source_line_number\": 67,\n      \"text\": \"          42.5.10    It is suggested to the contractor\
      \ that due care should be taken to select the peer\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 65,\n      \"source_line_number\"\
      : 68,\n      \"text\": \"                   educators from among the group of workmen so that they remain\
      \ with the contractor\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 65,\n      \"source_line_number\": 69,\n     \
      \ \"text\": \"                   throughout the contract period.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 65,\n\
      \      \"source_line_number\": 70,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 65,\n      \"\
      source_line_number\": 71,\n      \"text\": \"          42.6     Prevention of mosquito breeding\"\n    },\n\
      \    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 65,\n      \"source_line_number\": 72,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n\
      \      \"pdf_page\": 65,\n      \"source_line_number\": 73,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"\
      pdf_page\": 65,\n      \"source_line_number\": 74,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\"\
      : 65,\n      \"source_line_number\": 75,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n \
      \     \"source_line_number\": 3,\n      \"text\": \"42.6.1   Measures shall be taken to prevent mosquito breeding\
      \ at site. The measures to be\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\": 4,\n\
      \      \"text\": \"         taken shall include:\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\"\
      : 5,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\": 6,\n   \
      \   \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\": 7,\n      \"text\"\
      : \"                i)    Empty cans, oil drums, packing and other receptacles, which may retain water\"\n\
      \    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\": 8,\n      \"text\": \"            \
      \      shall be deposited at a central collection point and shall be removed from the\"\n    },\n    {\n \
      \     \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\": 9,\n      \"text\": \"                   site regularly.\"\
      \n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\": 10,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n\
      \    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\": 11,\n      \"text\": \"                  ii)\
      \      Still waters shall be treated at least once every week with oil in order to prevent\"\n    },\n   \
      \ {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\": 12,\n      \"text\": \"              mosquito\
      \ breeding.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\": 13,\n      \"text\":\
      \ \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\": 14,\n      \"text\": \"    \
      \              iii)    Contractor’s equipment and other items on the site, which may retain water,\"\n   \
      \ },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\": 15,\n      \"text\": \"              \
      \    shall be stored, covered or treated in such a manner that water could not be\"\n    },\n    {\n     \
      \ \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\": 16,\n      \"text\": \"                 retained.\"\n \
      \   },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\": 17,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n\
      \    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\": 18,\n      \"text\": \"             iv)   Water\
      \ storage tanks shall be provided.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\"\
      : 19,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\": 20,\n \
      \     \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\": 21,\n      \"\
      text\": \"42.6.2    Posters in both Hindi, English and local language which draw attention to the dangers\"\
      \n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\": 22,\n      \"text\": \"          \
      \ of permitting mosquito breeding, shall be displayed prominently on the site.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"\
      pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\": 23,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\"\
      : 66,\n      \"source_line_number\": 24,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n \
      \     \"source_line_number\": 25,\n      \"text\": \"42.6.3   The contractor at periodic interval shall arrange\
      \ to prevent mosquito breeding\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\": 26,\n\
      \      \"text\": \"        by fumigation  / spraying of insecticides. Most  effective insecticides  shall\"\
      \n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\": 27,\n      \"text\": \"         include\
      \ SOLFAC WP 10 or Baytex, The Ideal Larvicide etc.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\"\
      : 28,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\": 29,\n \
      \     \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\": 30,\n      \"\
      text\": \"42.7     Alcohol and drugs\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\"\
      : 31,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\": 32,\n \
      \     \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\": 33,\n      \"\
      text\": \"42.7.1   The contractor shall ensure at  all times that no employee  is working under the\"\n  \
      \  },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\": 34,\n      \"text\": \"          influence\
      \ of alcohol / drugs which are punishable under Govt. regulations.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n\
      \      \"source_line_number\": 35,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"\
      source_line_number\": 36,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\"\
      : 37,\n      \"text\": \"42.7.2   Smoking  at  public worksites by any employee  is also prohibited as per\
      \ Govt.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\": 38,\n      \"text\": \" \
      \         regulations.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\": 39,\n    \
      \  \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 66,\n      \"source_line_number\": 40,\n      \"text\"\
      : \"\"\n    }\n  ],\n  \"review_status\": \"pending_manual_review\",\n  \"title\": \"Medical Facilities\"\n\
      }"

    okf_sha256: b973183de77a23a5c4e6f7df14a6bfe6e19a3add3e80a6dc1db238991aff8dd3
    heading: Clause 43.0 — Noise
    source_document: Vol3DLNP1.pdf
    source_sha256: 1ee3fc8dfd12f659f34446b0a6db74e1e1cdd4d593ca91b2a48d59bf1ef97d52
    pdf_pages:
    - 66
    sheet_cells: ''
    json_path: parsed_json/vol3/part_3_health_welfare/health_welfare_clauses.json
    json_sha256: dc172436c564fe7d5a449f7f786e6863b16e68ebd59020d555002ab6fc04758d
    json_pointer: /clauses/2/fragments/0
    json_record_id: null
    source_review_status: pending_manual_review
    quote: "{\n  \"pdf_page\": 66,\n  \"text\": \"43.0     Noise\\n\\n\\n43.1    The Contractor shall consider noise\
      \ as an environmental constraint in his design,\\n          planning and execution of the Works and provide\
      \ demonstrable evidence of the same\\n        on Employer’s request. The Contractor shall, at his own expense,\
      \ take all appropriate\\n        measures  to ensure  that work  carried out by the Contractor and by  his\
      \ sub-\\n          Contractors, whether on or off the Site, will not cause any unnecessary or excessive\\\
      n         noise which may disturb the occupants of any nearby dwellings, schools, hospitals, or\\n       \
      \  premises with similar sensitivity to noise.\\n\\n\\n43.1.1    Without prejudice to the generality of the\
      \ foregoing, noise level reduction measures\\n           shall include the following:\\n\\n\\n           \
      \     i)    The Contractor shall ensure that all powered mechanical equipment used in the\\n             Works\
      \ shall be effectively sound reduced using the most modern techniques\\n                available including\
      \ but not limited to silencers and mufflers.\\n\\n                  ii)   The Contractor shall construct acoustic\
      \ screens or enclosures around any parts\\n                 of the Works from which excessive noise may be\
      \ generated.\\n\\n\\n43.1.2   The Contractor shall ensure that noise generated by work carried out by the\
      \ Contractor\\n        and his sub-Contractors during daytime and night time shall not exceed the maximum\\\
      n          permissible noise limits, whether continuously or intermittently, as given in the project\\n  \
      \     SHE Manual. The same may be varied from time to time by and at the sole discretion\\n           of the\
      \ Employer, In the event of a breach of this requirement, the Contractor shall\\n         immediately re-deploy\
      \ or adjust the relevant equipment or take other appropriate\\n        measures to reduce the noise levels\
      \ and thereafter maintain them at levels which do\\n          not exceed the said limits. Such measures may\
      \ include without limitation the temporary\\n          or permanent cessation of use of certain items of equipment.\"\
      \n}"
    page_basis: Parsed record provenance; not independently PDF-verified

      \        526.3864135742188,\n        285.5185852050781\n      ],\n      \"pdf_page\": 6,\n      \"text\":\
      \ \"Schedule-A. Tenderer has to select option “At Par / Excess / Less” and enter percentage\"\n    },\n  \
      \  {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        89.8787612915039,\n        288.84307861328125,\n        526.387451171875,\n\

      \ 89.87877655029297,\n        302.5230712890625,\n        526.3858642578125,\n        312.8785705566406\n\
      \      ],\n      \"pdf_page\": 6,\n      \"text\": \"select percentage option as ‘At Par’ only and for Schedule-C\
      \ tenderer shall offer Credit\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        89.87877655029297,\n        316.08306884765625,\n\
      \        378.5460510253906,\n        326.4385681152344\n      ],\n      \"pdf_page\": 6,\n      \"text\":\
      \ \"to BMRCL on Salvage of Materials, in ‘Lump sum’ amount.\"\n    }\n  ],\n  \"source_sha256\": \"d611ce86a28e84279e4cbbd66171d465a12d8e2efdaf37e6cac54cb5ee19246b\"\
      ,\n  \"text\": \"3. Tenderer shall quote in the Price Bid (.xls format) sheet only in accordance with the\
      \ ITT\\nclause 10. Tenderer is advised to quote their percentage rate in two decimals for\\nSchedule-A. Tenderer\

  - id: S10ec60af02b3e977
    okf_path: OKF_bundle/Financial/vol4_secJ.md
    okf_sha256: 5d2e43480b6c02206fddcc3a604c39f7a8b7e9151264f6fd244328f09f8e9c87
    heading: 1.0 — Pricing Document Preamble and Instructions
    source_document: Vol4DLNP1.pdf
    pdf_pages:
    - 6
    quote: "- **Read BOQ with the Complete Tender and Contract Documents** (Instruction 1)\n- **Provisional Quantities\
      \ and Payment for Measured Work** (Instruction 2)\n- **Price Bid Preparation in the Prescribed XLS Sheet**\
      \ (Instruction 3; ITT 10)\n  - Schedule-A: percentage rate to two decimals; At Par / Excess / Less\n  - Schedule-B:\
      \ At Par only\n  - Schedule-C: lump-sum salvage credit to BMRCL\n- **Proportionate Recovery and Adjustment\
      \ of Salvage Credit** (Instruction 4)\n- **Salvage Credit Based on Quoted Price** (Instruction 5)\n- **Taxes\
      \ and Statutory Levies Included, Except GST** (Instruction 6)\n- **Measurement of Completed Work** (Instruction\
      \ 7)\n- **Completed-Work Pricing and Included Execution Costs** (Instruction 8)\n- **Included Safety and Protection\
      \ Costs** (Instruction 9)\n- **Overheads, Profit, Risks and Defect-Remedy Obligations** (Instruction 10)\n\
      - **Accommodation, Insurance, Wages and Approval Costs** (Instruction 11)\n\n\n\n- **Execution Standards and\
      \ Engineer’s Directions** (Instruction 12)\n- **Incidental Removal Works Without Separate Payment** (Instruction\
      \ 13)\n- **Temporary Relocation and Protection of Utilities** (Instruction 14)\n- **Temporary Works, Power\
      \ and Supports at Contractor’s Cost** (Instruction 15)\n- **Permanent Supports and Restoration Payment Provisions**\
      \ (Instruction 16)\n- **Balcony and Canopy Area at 50%; Chejjas Excluded from Payment** (Instruction 17)\n\
      - **Demolition Depth and Refilling of Low-Level Areas** (Instruction 18)\n- **Removal Within the Line of Cut,\
      \ Excluding Trees** (Instruction 19)\n- **Total Floor Area as the Sum of Built Areas of All Floors** (Instruction\
      \ 20)\n- **Building Additions or Deletions and Accepted Percentage Rates** (Instruction 21)"
    page_basis: OKF reference, not independently PDF-verified
    page_precision: section_reference

- kind: summary
  text: Volume 4 pricing instructions impose specific financial and operational obligations on the contractor regarding
    utility relocation, temporary supports, permanent structural restoration using Common SR 2023-24, debris disposal
    at BBMP/GBA facilities, and salvage material credits.

    quote: "{\n  \"page_type\": \"text\",\n  \"pdf_page\": 6,\n  \"review_status\": \"pending_manual_review\",\n\
      \  \"text\": \" BMRCL/Phase-3/JPN-MRS/Demolition/DLN-P1/2026/157                                        Financial\
      \ Document \\n \\n \\n \\nBangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.                            September 2026\
      \ \\nSection Page 3 of 7 \\n \\nPricing Document-Preamble \\n1. The Pricing Document-Bill of Quantities (BOQ)\
      \ & Abstract to Bill of Quantities (BOQ) \\nshall be read in conjunction with the entire Tender and Contract\
      \ Document including \\nInstructions to Tenderers, General Conditions of Contract (including ‘Contract Data’),\
      \ \\nSpecial Conditions of Contract, Notice Inviting Tender, Technical Specifications, \\nEmployer’s Requirements,\
      \ Scope of work,  Schedule, Attachments, Appendix, Annexure, \\nCorrigendum and Addendums. \\n2. The quantities\
      \ given in the Price document - “Bill of Quantities” are estimated \\n(provisional). The basis of payment\
      \ will be the actual quantities of work ordered and \\ncarried out, as measured by the Contractor and verified\
      \ by the Engineer and valued at \\nthe rates in the accepted priced “Price Bid – Summary of Bill of Quantities”.\
      \ \\n3. Tenderer shall quote in the Price Bid (.xls format) sheet only in accordance with the ITT \\nclause\
      \ 10. Tenderer is advised to quote their percentage rate in two decimals for \\nSchedule-A. Tenderer has to\
      \ select option “At Par / Excess / Less” and enter percentage \\nrate in figures on estimated cost to Schedule-A,\
      \ for Schedule-B tenderer is required to \\nselect percentage option as ‘At Par’ only and for Schedule-C tenderer\
      \ shall offer Credit \\nto BMRCL on Salvage of Materials, in ‘Lump sum’ amount. \\n4. The Lump sum amount\
      \ indicated for Schedule C will be recovered from the running \\naccount bills proportionately. In case of\
      \ any variation in Quantities (increase/decrease), \\nthe value of credit to BMRCL will be increased/decreased\
      \ according to the value of work \\ndone from the ‘on account’ bills on pro-rata basis. \\n5. The Contractor\
      \ should note that the credit to BMRCL on salvage materials should be in \\nreference to the quoted price\
      \ and not on the estimate cost put to tender. \\n6. The ‘Tender Price’ or ‘Quoted Price’ includes all applicable\
      \ taxes, duties, cess, royalties, \\nand statutory levies as per the laws in force as on last day of the month\
      \ previous to the \\nmonth of invitation of this tender, except Goods and Services Tax (GST). \\n7. The method\
      \ of measurement of completed work for payment shall be in accordance with \\nthe requirements as stated in\
      \ the Technical Specifications and as given in the Price \\ndocument- Bill of Quantities or elsewhere in the\
      \ tender documents. \\n8. The quoted price is for completed and finished items of work and complete in all\
      \ respects \\ncomplying with the provisions of the Contract. It will be deemed to have included all \\nconstructional\
      \ plant, machinery tools, fuel, oil, consumables, electric power, water, \\ntransportation, disposal of debris\
      \ at BBMP/GBA identified C&D waste management \\nfacility (dismantled/demolished material), all leads & lifts,\
      \ all temporary works & false \\nworks, fencing, watering, lighting, maintenance, night working and inspection\
      \ facilities. \\n9. The quoted price will be deemed to have included all the safety measures at work sites/\
      \ \\ncasting yard, safety measures for workmen & road users, safety of the public and \\nprotection of adjoining\
      \ land & building while working. \\n10. The quoted price will be deemed to have included establishment & overhead\
      \ charges, \\ncontractor’s profit, supervision, other charges together with all general risks, liabilities\
      \ \\nand obligations set out or implied in the contract and including remedy of any defects \\nduring the\
      \ Defect Liability Period.  \\n11. The quoted price will be deemed to have included accommodations for staff\
      \ & workmen, \\nall insurance costs for labour & works, the payment of all wages, salaries, fees if any, \\\
      ncharges payable to various departments for obtaining necessary approvals if any. \\n305\\n\",\n  \"text_blocks\"\

    quote: "{\n  \"page_type\": \"text\",\n  \"pdf_page\": 7,\n  \"review_status\": \"pending_manual_review\",\n\
      \  \"text\": \" BMRCL/Phase-3/JPN-MRS/Demolition/DLN-P1/2026/157                                        Financial\
      \ Document \\n \\n \\n \\nBangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.                            September 2026\
      \ \\nSection Page 4 of 7 \\n \\n12. The work is to be executed as per Bill of Quantity description, Technical\
      \ Specifications, \\nspecifications of MORTH, KPWD, CPWD, drawings, best engineering practices and/or \\nas\
      \ directed by the Engineer.  \\n13. Removal of hoardings, interior lighting and interior furniture etc., ACP\
      \ cladding and \\nstructural glazing etc., which are existing within the line of cut are incidental to work.\
      \  No \\nseparate payment will be considered for payment. \\n14. If it is necessary to maintain any power,\
      \ water or other utilities during demolition, such \\nlines shall be temporarily relocated, as necessary and\
      \ protected before commencement \\nof demolition work.   \\n15. All such cost involved for temporary relocation/maintenance\
      \ must be borne by contractor \\nand no payment will be made for temporary works. The contractor shall make\
      \ his own \\narrangements for power requirement for his equipment/machinery. If advised by the \\nConsultant/BMRCL,\
      \ the contractor has to give temporary supports for the building during \\ndemolition wherever necessary without\
      \ any extra financial implications to BMRCL. \\n16. If advised by the Consultant/BMRCL, the contractor has\
      \ to give permanent supports like \\nwall, columns (Steel, RCC, etc.), including plastering, painting, plumbing,\
      \ water supply \\nand sanitary works, electrical restoration works, etc. to the supported portion of the \\\
      nbuilding before or after total/partial demolition, wherever necessary to maintain LOC, for \\nwhich payment\
      \ will be made as per  accepted rate in BoQ schedule for items available in \\nCommon SR for the year 2023-24\
      \ (Schedule of Rates),  for the execution of such work.  \\n17. 50% area of Balcony and Canopy if exists will\
      \ be considered for calculating area for \\npayment as per the guide lines for valuation of properties. The\
      \ area of chejjas or such \\nsimilar coverings/shadows will not be considered for payment.  \\n18. The demolition\
      \ and dismantling shall be carried out up to ground and/or up to basement \\nlevel as the case may be including\
      \ underground water storage structures if any. Refilling \\nthe low-level areas up to ground level is within\
      \ the scope of contractor’s work.  \\n19. The items existing excluding trees within the proposed line of cut\
      \ have to be dismantled \\nand demolished and debris to be transported and disposed. \\n20. The total floor\
      \ area means the summation of total built area of all floors in the buildings \\nindicated in Schedule A.\
      \ \\n21. In case some buildings are deleted or added in BOQ, the rates per sqm for that \\ndeletion/addition\
      \ will be same as given above. For payment purpose, the %age quoted \\nand accepted in the Summary Cost will\
      \ be used for the above rate.  \\n \\n \\n \\n \\n \\n \\n \\n \\n \\n \\n \\n \\n \\n306\\n\",\n  \"text_blocks\"\

  text: Under price bid declarations, submission of incorrect information or suppression of material facts authorizes
    BMRCL to reject future tenders, cancel awarded contracts without financial claims or arbitration, and remove
    the agency from the qualified panel, in addition to GCC Clause 4.33 provisions.

- kind: summary
  text: Corrigendum No. 2 introduces new Special Conditions of Contract (SCC Clauses 4 through 10) which explicitly
    state that entire GCC subclauses regarding sub-contractors, early completion incentives, price variations, mobilization
    advances, plant & machinery advances, special/acceleration advances, and professional indemnity insurance stand
    deleted.

  - id: J43ced15c147bcb43
    okf_path: OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol1/secB.md
    okf_sha256: e3cf38f3c94155bb807c3dc1374cc20588ab715e06455bf88cda54b8e92b1665
    heading: Clause 5.0 — Contents of Tender Documents
    source_document: Vol1DLNP1.pdf
    source_sha256: 74441e621d2e627d9132506a894883aff3b5fe3fdfa9600f5be3c7ec394d5c5c
    pdf_pages:
    - 12
    sheet_cells: ''
    json_path: parsed_json/vol1/section_b_itt/itt_clauses.json
    json_sha256: 5cbf8e397b40de4d6a1dfcd47405f4c93e0487e11869bb7807e45ebef78c56bf
    json_pointer: /clauses/4
    json_record_id: null
    source_review_status: pending_manual_review
    quote: "{\n  \"clause_id\": \"5.0\",\n  \"fragments\": [\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 12,\n      \"text\": \"\
      5.0     CONTENTS OF TENDER DOCUMENTS\\n\\n         5.1      The Tender Documents as listed below have been\
      \ prepared for inviting tenders:\\n\\n\\n\\n                                         CONTENT\\n\\n       \
      \                                      Technical Document (Volume -1)\\n\\n                              \
      \   Section -A     Notice Inviting Tender (NIT)\\n                                 Section -B      Instructions\
      \ to Tenderers (ITT)\\n                                 Section -C       Eligibility cum Qualification Criteria\
      \ (EQC)\\n                                 Section -D      Letter of Technical Bid (Including Attachments)\\\
      n\\n                                             General Conditions of Contract (GCC) including\\n       \
      \                          Section -E\\n                                                Contract Data\\n \
      \                                Section -F      Special Conditions of Contract (SCC)\\n                 \
      \                Section -G     Contract Forms\\n\\n                                            Technical\
      \ Document (Volume -2)\\n\\n                                Section-H     Employer’s Requirements & Technical\
      \ Specifications\\n\\n                                            Technical Document (Volume -3)\\n      \
      \                              Section-I       Safety, Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\\n\\n           \
      \                           Financial Document (Pricing Document) (Volume -4)\\n\\n                      \
      \                                Letter of Price Bid\\n\\n                                           Preamble\\\
      n                                 Section -J\\n                                                          \
      \           Bill of Quantities (BOQ)\\n\\n                                     Summary of Bill of Quantities\
      \ (.xls)\\n\\n\\n\\n         5.2      The Tenderer is expected to examine carefully all instructions, forms,\
      \ terms, conditions,\\n                      specifications, and other contents of the Tender Documents, and\
      \ to take them fully into\\n                   account before submitting the Tender. Failure to comply with\
      \ any of the requirements or\\n                     provisions set forth in the Tender Documents shall be\
      \ entirely at the Tenderer’s own risk.\"\n    }\n  ],\n  \"heading_line\": \"5.0     CONTENTS OF TENDER DOCUMENTS\"\
      ,\n  \"review_status\": \"pending_manual_review\"\n}"
    page_basis: Parsed record provenance; not independently PDF-verified

- kind: summary
  text: During technical evaluation, BMRCL may request clarifications in writing through the eProcurement Portal,
    but respondents are barred from submitting additional documents unrelated to the specific clarification sought.
  evidence_ids:
  - Jecec2d2b1a0fba3a
  relation_type: none
  explicitness: not_a_relation
  from_id: ''
  to_id: ''
  id: C79b3a694c1b9311a
  citations:
  - id: Jecec2d2b1a0fba3a
    okf_path: OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol1/secB.md
    okf_sha256: e3cf38f3c94155bb807c3dc1374cc20588ab715e06455bf88cda54b8e92b1665
    heading: Clause 20.0 — Clarification of Tenders
    source_document: Vol1DLNP1.pdf
    source_sha256: 74441e621d2e627d9132506a894883aff3b5fe3fdfa9600f5be3c7ec394d5c5c
    pdf_pages:
    - 20
    sheet_cells: ''
    json_path: parsed_json/vol1/section_b_itt/itt_clauses.json
    json_sha256: 5cbf8e397b40de4d6a1dfcd47405f4c93e0487e11869bb7807e45ebef78c56bf
    json_pointer: /clauses/19
    json_record_id: null
    source_review_status: pending_manual_review
    quote: "{\n  \"clause_id\": \"20.0\",\n  \"fragments\": [\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 20,\n      \"text\": \"\
      20.0     CLARIFICATION OF TENDERS\\n\\n         20.1       Evaluation of Technical Bids submitted by Tenderers\
      \ shall be undertaken based on details\\n                   submitted in the Technical Bids only. Employer\
      \ may at his discretion ask any Tenderer for\\n                        clarification on his submittals through\
      \ eProcurement portal. The Tenderer, in response to the\\n                         clarification, is not permitted\
      \ to submit additional document not pertaining to the clarification\\n                    sought. The request\
      \ for clarification and the response shall be in writing only through\\n                  eProcurement Portal\
      \ and no response leading to material change in the submittals will be\\n                   taken into consideration.\
      \  It is not mandatory for Employer to seek clarification. Employer\\n                   reserves the right\
      \ to evaluate the bids based on documents submitted by the bidders.\\n\\n         20.2       Notwithstanding\
      \ the provision in Para 20.1 above, the Tenderer must take care that all\\n                    required documents\
      \  to  establish  their  Eligibility &  Qualification and other mandatory\\n                   requirements\
      \ must be submitted before the deadline for submission.\\n\\n         20.3      During the evaluation of financial\
      \ bid, the Employer may ask Tenderers individually for\\n                        clarification regarding their\
      \ price bids, including the breakup of prices. The request for\\n                        clarification and\
      \ the response shall be in writing and no change in the price or substance of\\n                    the Price\
      \ bid shall be sought, offered, or permitted except as required to confirm the correction\\n             \
      \         of arithmetical errors discovered by the Employer during the evaluation of Tenders in\\n       \
      \           accordance with Clause 22.2.5.\\n\\n         20.4           If a Bidder does not provide clarifications\
      \ of his bid by the date and time set in the Employer’s\\n                    request for clarification, their\
      \ bid shall be evaluated as per the available information in the\\n                   submitted bid.\"\n \
      \   }\n  ],\n  \"heading_line\": \"20.0     CLARIFICATION OF TENDERS\",\n  \"review_status\": \"pending_manual_review\"\
      \n}"
    page_basis: Parsed record provenance; not independently PDF-verified
    heading_alignment: exact_number_match
  created_at: '2026-11-01T06:32:09+00:00'
  status: draft
  editorial_history:

- kind: summary
  text: ITT 20.2 requires documents establishing eligibility and qualification, and other mandatory requirements,
    to be submitted before the deadline. Under ITT 20.1, the Employer may request clarification but is not obliged
    to do so; additional documents unrelated to the clarification sought are not permitted, and responses leading
    to material changes are not considered. These provisions do not by themselves support a blanket statement that
    every initial omission is incapable of clarification.
  evidence_ids:
  - Jecec2d2b1a0fba3a
  relation_type: none
  explicitness: not_a_relation
  from_id: ''
  to_id: ''
