>       "pdf_page": 27,
>       "text": "2.0     ELIGIBLE TENDERERS\n\n\n       2.1.   Tenderer may be a Proprietorship Firm/ Partnership Firm/Private Limited Company/ Public\n              Limited Company. Tenderer shall apply as a Single Entity. Joint Venture/Consortium is\n            not permitted in this tender.\n\n       2.2.   Only those Tenderers who satisfy the condition of ‘Class-I Local Supplier’ as per Clause\n              2.3 of EQC shall be eligible to participate in this Tender.\n\n       2.3.    ‘Class-I Local Supplier’ means a supplier or service provider whose goods, services or works\n              offered for procurement, meets the minimum Local content of 90%.\n\n       2.4.    ‘Local content’ means the amount of value added in India which shall, be the total value of\n              the item procured (excluding net domestic indirect taxes) minus the value of imported content\n                 in the item (including all customs duties) as proportion of the total value, in percent.\n\n       2.5.   The minimum Local Content for this service shall be certified as below;\n\n                (a) The ‘Class-I Local Supplier’ at the time of Bid submission shall provide a Declaration\n                    that the item offered meets the minimum ‘Local Content’ and shall give details of the\n                    location(s) at which the local value addition is made. (Attachment-1 to Letter of Technical\n                    Bid).\n\n                (b) DELETED\n\n       2.6.   Bidder claiming as ‘Class-I Local Supplier is exempted from meeting the stipulated\n             ‘Local content’ in case, if the product is being manufactured in India under a license from a\n               foreign manufacturer, who holds intellectual property rights and where there is a technology\n               collaboration agreement/transfer of technology agreement for indigenous manufacture of a\n             product developed abroad with clear phasing of increase in local content, subject to\n             submission of necessary documents.\n\n       2.7.   Bidder  shall note  that  in case  of ‘Imported Products’, the related services such as\n               transportation, insurance,  installation, commissioning, training and after sales services"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 28,
>       "text": "support like AMC/CMC etc., shall not be claimed as Local value addition. Such services will\n              not be considered as ‘Local Content’.\n\n       2.8.   A committee  with  Internal and  external experts  for Independent  verification  of  self-\n              Declarations by the Bidder may be constituted by BMRCL on random basis and in case of\n              complaints.\n\n       2.9.    In case of false declarations by a Bidder claiming as Class-I Local Supplier, the following\n              actions will be taken;\n\n                (a) The proposal submitted by the Bidder will be rejected.\n\n                (b) The Bidder will be debarred for up to Two years.\n\n       2.10.  Any Bidder from a country which shares a land border with India will be eligible to bid in this\n             Bid only  if the Bidder  is registered with the Competent Authority. Further, any bidder\n               (including from India) having specified Transfer of Technology (ToT) arrangement with an\n                entity from a country which share a land border with India, shall also require to be registered\n              with the same competent authority constituted by Department for Promotion of Industry and\n               Internal Trade (DPIIT), Government of India or the Competent Authority constituted by\n            Government of Karnataka.\n\n                          i.  \"Bidder\" (including the term 'Bidder', 'consultant' or 'service provider' in certain contexts)\n             means any person or firm or company, including any member of a consortium or joint\n                venture (that is an association of several persons, or firms or companies), every artificial\n                    juridical person not falling in any of the descriptions of Bidders stated hereinbefore,\n                  including any agency branch or office controlled by such person, participating in a\n               procurement process.\n\n                          ii.  \"Bidder from a country which shares a land border with India” means:\n\n                    (a) An entity incorporated, established or registered in such a country; or\n\n                    (b) A subsidiary of an entity incorporated, established or registered in such a country; or\n\n                     (c) An  entity  substantially  controlled through  entities  incorporated,  established  or\n                      registered in such a country; or\n\n                    (d) An entity whose beneficial owner is situated in such a country; or\n\n                    (e) An Indian (or other) agent of such an entity; or\n\n                         (f) A natural person who is a citizen of such a country; or\n\n                    (g) A consortium or Joint venture where any member of the consortium or joint venture\n                           falls under any of the above.\n\n                          iii. The beneficial owner for the purpose of (ii) above will be as under:\n\n                    (a) In case of a company or Limited Liability Partnership, the beneficial owner is the\n                      natural person(s), who, whether acting alone or together, or through one or more\n                          juridical person, has a controlling ownership interest or who exercises control through\n                     other means.\n\n                         \"Controlling ownership interest\" means ownership of or entitlement to more than\n                           twenty-five per cent of shares or capital or profits of the company;"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 29,
>       "text": "   \"Control” shall include the right to appoint majority of the directors or to control\n                         the management or policy decisions including by virtue of their shareholding or\n                    management rights or shareholders  agreements  or voting agreements;\n                    (b) In case of a partnership firm, the beneficial owner is the natural person(s) who,\n                   whether acting alone or together, or through one or more juridical person, has\n                   ownership of entitlement to more than fifteen percent of capital or profits of the\n                      partnership;\n\n                     (c) In case of an unincorporated association or body of individuals, the beneficial owner\n                           is the natural person(s), who, whether acting alone or together, or through one or\n                 more juridical person, has ownership of or entitlement to more than fifteen percent of\n                     the property or capital or profits of such association or body of individuals;\n\n                    (d) Where no natural person is identified under (a) or (b) or (c) above, the beneficial\n                  owner is the relevant natural person who holds the position of senior managing\n                            official;\n\n                    (e) In case of a trust, the identification  of beneficial  owner(s) shall include identification\n                       of the author of the trust, the trustee, the beneficiaries with fifteen percent or more\n                        interest in the trust and any other natural person exercising ultimate effective control\n                    over the trust through a chain of control or ownership.\n\n                         (f) An Agent is a person employed to do any act for another, or to represent another in\n                     dealings with third person.\n\n                                      i. Successful Bidder shall not be allowed to sub-contract works to any contractor from\n                  a country which shares a land border with India unless such contractor is registered\n                       with the Competent Authority. The definition of “Contractor from a country which\n                     shares a land border with India” shall be as in para (ii) above.\n                                       ii. The registration shall be valid at the time of submission of bids and at the time of\n                    acceptance of bid.\n                                       iii. If the bidder was validly registered at the time of acceptance / placement of order,\n                         registration shall not be relevant consideration during contract execution.\n       2.11.  Bidder  is required  to submit undertaking  for compliance  of the above Clause 2.10\n             (Attachment-1 to Letter of Technical Bid).\n\n       2.12.  The above Clause 2.10 will not apply to Bidders from those countries (even if sharing a land\n             border with India) to which the Government of India has extended lines of credit or in which\n              the Government of India is engaged in development projects. Updated lists of countries to\n             which lines of credit have been extended or in which development projects are undertaken\n             are given in the website of the Ministry of External Affairs.\n\n       2.13.  Tenderers shall not have a conflict of interest that affects the Bidding Process. Any Tenderer\n             found to have a Conflict of Interest shall be disqualified. A Tenderer will be deemed to have\n               Conflict of interest with one or more parties in this Tendering process, if:\n\n                (a) A tenderer submits more than one bid either individually or as a member of a JV for a\n                work will result in disqualification of all its bids for that work.\n\n                (b) The Tenderer has been engaged by the Employer to provide consulting services for the\n                  preparation related to procurement for or implementation of this work.\n\n                 (c) Any association/affiliation (inclusive of parent firms) of the Bidder has been engaged by\n                  the Employer to provide consulting services for the preparation related to procurement"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 30,
>       "text": "for or implementation of this work.\n\n                (d) The Tenderer lends, or temporarily seconds its personnel to Firms or organizations\n                which are engaged in Consulting services for the preparation related to procurement for\n                   or implementation of this work.\n\n       2.14.  DELETED\n\n       2.15.  As on date of submission of Bid, the Bidder (each member, in case of JV) should not be\n             excluded as per the GROUNDS FOR EXCLUSION. The Bidder should submit an\n              undertaking to this effect.\n\n                      (i) GROUNDS FOR EXCLUSION will constitute the following, if the Bidders (in case of JV\n                 applicable to all member(s));\n\n                  (a) are bankrupt, being wound up or ceasing their activities, are having their activities\n                   administered by the courts, have entered into receivership, or are in any analogous\n                      situation arising from any similar procedure; or\n\n                         is under insolvency proceedings at the time of bid submission and until the contract is\n                awarded or adjudged insolvent by the National Company Law Tribunal (NCLT), with\n                 any resolution proceedings pending before the NCLT;\n\n                  (b) have been convicted within the past five years by a court decision, which has the force\n                      of residential jurisdiction in the country where the project is implemented, of fraud or\n                    corruption or any other offence committed during the procurement or performance of a\n                     contract, unless they provide supporting information together with their Statement of\n                       Integrity which shows that this conviction is not relevant in the context of this project;\n\n                   (c) have committed serious professional misconduct within the past five years during the\n                 Procurement or performance of a contract, as evidenced by any means by the\n                  Employer;\n\n                  (d) have not fulfilled their obligations regarding the payment of social security contributions\n                    or taxes in accordance with the legal provisions of the country where they are\n                    established or the Employer ‘s country;\n\n                  (e) have indulged in ‘Corrupt Practice’; “corrupt practice” means the offering, giving,\n                    receiving or soliciting of anything of value to influence the action of a public official in\n                   the procurement process or in contract execution;\n\n                       (f) have indulged in ‘Fraudulent Practice’; “fraudulent practice” means a misrepresentation\n                      of facts in order to influence an evaluation process or the execution of a contract, and\n                    includes misleading or false representation in forms or statements or attachment\n                   submitted and collusive practices among Bidders (prior to or after bid submission)\n                  designed to establish bid prices at artificial, non-competitive levels and to deprive the\n                 Employer of the benefits of free and open competitions;\n\n                  (g) have been considered ineligible due to execution of Bid–Securing Declaration by any\n                    Central Govt./State Govt./PSUs;\n\n                  (h) have been Black  listed/debarred /banned business by any Central Govt./State\n                 Govt./PSUs or Local Authority or World Bank or ADB or JICA or any other International\n                   Financing Institution, or any entity controlled by them;\n\n                         (i) have left/abandoned the awarded works on his own in the last 05 years;"

### Jff563a5330de75d8

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol1/secE#Clause 4 — The Contractor|Clause 4 — The Contractor]]

> {
>   "pdf_page": 88,
>   "text": "Protection   of  4.17       The Contractor shall take all reasonable steps to protect the environment (both on\n     the                       and off the Site) and to avoid injury, damage and nuisance to people and property\n    Environment                   resulting from pollution, noise and other results of his operations. The Contractor\n                                        shall ensure that air emissions, surface discharges and effluent from the Site\n                                    during the Contract Period shall not exceed the values indicated in the Employer's\n                                Requirements & Technical  specifications, and  shall not exceed the values\n                                    prescribed by law. The Contractor shall conform to the Employer’s Requirements\n                         & Technical specifications and shall indemnify the Employer against any liability\n                                     or damages or claims arising out of his operations. The Contractor shall be\n                                    responsible and liable for any stoppage, closure or suspension of the works due\n                                        to any contravention of statutory requirements relating to the protection of the\n                                 environment and shall indemnify and keep indemnified the Employer in this\n                                     regard.\n                                     Outline Environmental Plan means the environmental plan forming part of the\n                                  Tender, setting out,  in summary form, the Contractor's proposed means of\n                                 complying  with  his  obligations  in  relation  to  environmental  quality.  Site\n                                  Environmental Plan means the site environmental plan including all supplements\n                                       thereto, or any amended or varied version thereof, as submitted by the Contractor\n                                           in accordance with Employer’s Safety, Health and Environmental Manual (SHE\n                                  Manual), this Clause and which has received the Engineer’s consent. The Site\n                                  Environmental Plan shall include detailed policies, procedures and regulations\n                                   which, when implemented,  will ensure compliance  with  this  Clause. The\n                                    Contractor is required to make himself aware of  all the requirements of the\n                                 Employer’s SHE Manual in this regard and comply with them.\n\n                             The Contractor's Site Environmental Plan shall be developed from his Employer’s\n                                     Safety. Health and Environmental Manual (SHE Manual), as per the Employer's\n                                 Requirements.\n\n                       4.17.1      Within stipulated time as mentioned in ‘contract data’, the Contractor shall submit\n                              a detailed and comprehensive Site Environmental Plan based on the Employer’s\n                                     Safety, Health and Environmental Manual (SHE Manual), and shall include such\n                                        further material, which the Contractor considers necessary and relevant.\n\n                            Upon the Engineer notifying his consent to the Site Environmental Plan, or any\n                                 supplemental part thereof, the Contractor shall adhere to the principles and\n                                 procedures contained in such document save to the extent that the Engineer may\n                                    give his consent to any amended or varied version thereof.\n                             The Contractor shall provide all necessary access, assistance and facilities to\n                                 enable the Engineer and the Employer to monitor and conduct tests to verify that\n                                    the Site Environmental Plan is being properly and fully implemented.\n\n      Electricity,       4.18      The Contractor shall be responsible for making his own arrangements at his own\n    Water and Gas                cost to obtain supply of water, electricity or gas for the Works. The Employer where\n                                       feasible may at his discretion assist the Contractor in giving recommendatory\n                                           letters etc.\n     Tools, Plants     4.19       Except for any specific item mentioned in the Special Conditions of Contract or in\n    And Equipment              Employer’s Requirements, the Contractor shall provide  all tools, plants and\n    Supplied By                Equipment  for the Works.  In respect  of such exceptional  tools, plants  or\n    The Employer              Equipment committed to be provided by the Employer under terms and conditions\n                                      specified in the Special Conditions of Contract, the Contractor shall take  all\n                                  reasonable care and shall be responsible for all damages or loss caused by him,\n                                       his representatives, sub-contractors or his workmen or others while they are in his\n                                   charge.\n                          No  tools, plant and equipment shall be supplied by the Employer. Unless\n                                         specifically incorporated in special conditions, the Contractor has to arrange all\n                                          tools, plant, equipment required for the work.\n                         On completion of the Works, the Contractor shall hand over the unused balance\n                                        of the tools, plants and Equipment supplied by the Employer to the Employer back\n                                           in good order and repair, fair wear and tear expected, and shall be responsible for\n                               any failure to account for the same or any damage done thereto.\n                             The decision of the Engineer as to the amount recoverable from the Contractor on\n                                            this account shall be final and binding."

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol1/secE#Clause 17 — Claims, Disputes and Conciliation|Clause 17 — Claims, Disputes and Conciliation]]

>       "text": "17         Claims, Disputes and Conciliation\n                         Any and all Disputes shall be settled in accordance with the provision of this\n                           Clause\nProcedure for     17.1             If the Contractor intends to claim any additional payment under any clause of\nClaims                      these Conditions or otherwise, the Contractor shall give notice to the Engineer as\n                          soon as possible and in any event within 28 days of the start of the event giving\n                                   rise to the claim.\n\n                         The Contractor shall keep such contemporary records as may be necessary to\n                                substantiate any claim, either on the Site or at any other location acceptable to the\n                              Engineer. Without admitting the Employer’s liability, the Engineer shall, on receipt\n                                  of such notice, inspect such records and may instruct the Contractor to keep\n                                  further contemporary records. The Contractor shall permit the Engineer to inspect\n                                           all such records, and shall (if instructed) submit copies to the Engineer.\n\n\n                               Within 28 days of such notice, or such other time as may be agreed by the\n                              Engineer, the Contractor shall send to the Engineer a fully detailed claim which\n                               includes full supporting particulars of the basis of the claim and additional payment\n                               claimed. If the event or circumstance giving rise to the claim has a continuing\n                                    effect:\n\n                                  a.  the initial fully detailed claim shall be considered as interim;\n\n                                  b.  the Contractor shall send further interim claims at monthly intervals, giving the\n                                  cumulative amount claimed, and any such further particulars as the Engineer\n                         may reasonably require; and\n\n                                    c.  the contractor shall send a final claim within 28 days after the end of effects\n                                      resulting from the event or circumstance, or within such other period as may\n                             be proposed by the Contractor and approved by the Engineer.\n\n                                                   If the Contractor fails to comply with this Sub-clause, he shall not be entitled to\n                               claim any additional payment.\n\nPayment for      17.2      The Contractor  shall be  entitled  to have included  in any Interim Payment\nClaims                           Certificate such amount for any claim as the Engineer considers due, after taking\n                              approval from  the Employer.  If the  particulars supplied are  insufficient  to\n                                substantiate the whole of the claim, the Contractor shall be entitled to payment for\n                           such part of the claim as has been substantiated.\n\nNo Legal         17.3      Any and all Disputes shall be settled in accordance with the provisions of Clause\naction till                     17. No action at law concerning or arising out of any Dispute shall be commenced\nDispute                      unless and until all applicable Dispute resolution procedures set out in Clause 17\nSettlement                      shall have been finally exhausted in relation to that Dispute or any Dispute out of\nProcedure is                which that Dispute shall have arisen with which  it may be or may have been\nExhausted                   connected.\n\nNotice of         17.4     A Dispute shall be deemed to arise when one party serves a notice in writing\nDispute                         (hereinafter called a \"Notice of Dispute\") within 30 days from the date of rejection\n                                  of claim by the parties.\n\n\nDispute           17.5       Disputes shall be settled through Conciliation procedures as established by “The\nResolution                      Arbitration and Conciliation Act, 1996” and as amended from time to time.\n\nConciliation      17.6        Within 60 days of receipt of Notice of Dispute, either party shall refer the matter in\n                               dispute to conciliation. Conciliation proceedings shall be initiated within 30 days\n                                  of one party  inviting the other  in  writing  to  Conciliation.  Conciliation  shall\n                       commence when the other party accepts in writing this invitation.  If the invitation\n                                        is not accepted, then Conciliation shall not take place.    If the party initiating\n                                   conciliation does not receive a reply within 30 days from the date on which he\n                           sends the invitation, he may elect to treat this as a rejection of the invitation to\n                                  conciliate and inform the other party accordingly.\n                         The Conciliation shall be undertaken by one Conciliator selected from a panel of\n                                 Conciliators maintained by the Employer. The Conciliator shall assist the parties\n                                  to reach an amicable settlement in an independent and impartial manner."
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 140,
>       "text": "Conciliation      17.7      The Employer shall maintain a panel of Conciliators with requisite qualifications\n    Procedure                 and professional experience who shall be from serving or retired engineers of\n                              Government Departments, or of Public Sector Undertakings. Panel  list not to\n                                     include serving or former employees of the Employer. Out of this panel, a list of\n                                    three Conciliators shall be sent to the Contractor who shall choose one of them to\n                                     act as Conciliator and conduct conciliation proceedings in accordance with “The\n                                        Arbitration and Conciliation Act, 1996”, of India. The party serving notice of dispute\n                              on the other party shall also serve such notice on the Conciliator chosen as per\n                                           this Clause. The Employer at the time of offering the panel of Conciliators to be\n                                  appointed as Conciliator shall also supply the information with regard to the\n                                        qualifications of the said Conciliators nominated in the panel along with their\n                                     professional experience, phone nos. and addresses to the Contractor.\n\n                                        In the event of the Contractor not choosing the Conciliator from the panel furnished\n                               by the Employer, within a period of 30 days, the Employer is at liberty to choose\n                              and nominate a Conciliator from the same panel and communicate to the\n                                    Contractor which will be final and binding on the Contractor.\n\n\n                             The Employer and the Contractor  shall  in good  faith co-operate  with the\n                                       Conciliator and, in particular, shall endeavour to comply with requests by the\n                                       Conciliator to submit written materials, provide evidence and attend meetings.\n                             Each party may, on his own initiative or at the invitation of the Conciliator, submit\n                                        to the Conciliator suggestions for the settlement of the dispute.\n\n\n                         When it appears to the Conciliator that there exist elements of a settlement which\n                          may be acceptable to the parties, he shall formulate the terms of a possible\n                                    settlement and submit them to the parties for their observations. After receiving\n                                    the observations of the parties, the Conciliator may reformulate the terms of a\n                                    possible settlement in the light of such observations.\n\n\n                                                            If the Parties reach agreement on a settlement of the dispute, they may draw up\n                              and sign a  written settlement agreement.  If requested by the Parties, the\n                                       Conciliator may draw up, or assist the Parties in drawing up, the settlement\n                                 agreement.\n                         When the Parties sign the settlement agreement, it shall be final and binding on\n                                    the parties and persons claiming under them respectively.\n                             The Conciliator shall authenticate the settlement agreement and furnish a copy\n                                     thereof to each of the Parties.\n\n\n                            To the fullest extent feasible, the conciliatory process must be concluded within a\n\n                                    period of sixty days subsequent to the acknowledgment of the appointment notice\n\n                               by the Conciliator.\n\n                             The Parties shall not initiate, during the conciliation proceedings, any judicial\n                                  proceedings in respect of a dispute that is the subject matter of the conciliation\n                                   proceedings.\n\n    Termination of    17.8      The conciliation proceedings shall be terminated:\n     Conciliation                    a.  by the signing of the settlement agreement by the Parties on the date of\n    Proceedings                    agreement; or\n                                        b.  by written declaration of the Conciliator, after consultation with the Parties, to\n                                        the effect further efforts at conciliation are no longer justified, on the date of\n                                           declaration; or\n                                          c.  by a written declaration of the Parties to the Conciliator to the effect that the\n                                             conciliation proceedings are terminated, on the date of declaration; or\n                                        d.  by a written declaration of a Party to the other Party and the Conciliator,  if\n                                       appointed, to the effect that the conciliation proceedings are terminated, on\n                                        the date of declaration.\n                            Upon termination of the conciliation proceedings, the Conciliator shall fix the costs\n                                        of the conciliation and give written notice thereof to the Parties. The costs shall be\n                                borne equally by the Parties unless settlement agreement provides for a different\n                                   apportionment. All other expenses incurred by a Party shall be borne by that Party."

Neither party shall\n                          be entitled to suspend the work or part of the work to which the dispute relates on\n                            account of conciliation and payments to the Contractor shall continue to be made\n                                     in terms of the Contract.\n\nJudicial          17.10        In the event that a dispute remains unresolved via the conciliation process, the\nRecourses                   aggrieved party may seek judicial recourses.\n\nJurisdiction      17.11      Any suit or application, arising out of any dispute or differences on account of this\nof Courts                      contract shall be filed in a competent court at Bangalore, Karnataka only and no\n                               other court or any other district of the country shall have any jurisdiction on the\n                                matter.\n\nMatters finally    17.12            If the Contractor (s) does/do not prefer his/ their specific and final claims in writing,\ndetermined by                 within a period of 90 days of receiving the intimation from the Employer that the\nthe Employer                      final bill is ready for payment, he/they will be deemed to have waived his/their\n                              claims (s) and the Employer shall be discharged and released of all liabilities under\n                               the contract in respect of these claims."

   ii)   The name(s) and experience of person(s) within the Contractor’s proposed\n            management who shall be responsible for co-ordinating and monitoring the\n                Contractor’s SHE performance;\n\n                  iii)   The number  of SHE  staff who  shall be employed on  the Works,  their\n                  responsibilities,  authority  and  line  of  communication  with  the  proposed\n                Contractor's agent;\n\n             iv)   A statement  of the Contractor’s policy and procedures  for  identifying and\n                estimating hazards, and the provided by the Contractor, with an outline of the syllabus to be followed;\n\n              vii)   Details of the safety equipment which shall be provided by the Contractor,\n                including personal protective equipment;\n\n               viii)  A statement of the Contractor’s policy and procedures for ensuring that\n                Contractor's Equipment used on the Project Site are maintained in a safe\n                condition and are operated in a safe manner;\n\n             ix)  A statement of the Contractor’s policy and procedures for ensuring that sub-\n                contractors comply with the Contractor's safety plan;\n\n           x)   A statement of the Contractor’s disciplinary procedures with respect to SHE\n                 related matters, and\n\n             xi)  A statement of the Contractor’s procedure for reporting and investigating\n                accidents, dangerous occurrences or occupational illnesses\n\n\n4.3     The Contractor shall, from time to time and as necessary are required by the\n         Employer to produce supplements to the SHE Plan such that  it is at all times a\n           detailed, comprehensive and contemporaneous statement by the Contractor of his\n            site safety, industrial health and environment obligations, responsibilities, policies\n        and procedures relating to work on Site. Any and all submissions of supplements\n           to the SHE Plan shall be made to the Employer in accordance with the agreed\n         procedures.\n\n4.4           If at any time the SHE plan is, in the Employer’s opinion, insufficient or requires\n           revision or modification to ensure the security of the Works and the safety of all\n        workmen upon and visitors to the Site, the Employer may instruct the Contractor\n           to revise the SHE plan and the Contractor shall within 7 days submit the revised\n          plan to the Employer for review.\n\n\n4.5     Any omissions, inconsistencies and errors in the SHE Plan or the Employer’s\n         acceptance or rejection of the SHE Plan and/or supplements thereto shall be\n          without prejudice  to the Contractor's  obligations with respect  to  site  safety,\n           industrial health and environment and  shall not excuse any  failure by the\n          contractor  to adopt proper and recognised  safety  practices throughout  the\n          execution of the Work.\n\n\n4.6     The Contractor  shall adhere  to the SHE Plan and  shall ensure, as  far as\n           practically possible, that  all sub-contractors of  all tiers require that contracting\n           parties each have a copy of the Site SHE Plan and comply with its provisions.\n\n\n4.7     The details of contents to be covered in the site SHE plan are given in Appendix\n         No.: 3."
>     }

>       "pdf_page": 12,
>       "text": "3.0      Compliance\n\n3.1     Memorandum of Understanding (MOU)\n\n\n3.1.1   A Memorandum of Understanding placed at Appendix No.: 1 shall be executed after\n          the award of contract before commencement of work by the contractor with regard to\n          various provisions on Safety, Health and Environment to be practiced during the\n          construction work.\n\n\n3.2     BMRC’s SHE Policy and Management Systems\n\n\n3.2.1    The construction works shall be undertaken in accordance with BMRC’s SHE Policy\n        and Management Systems as amended from time to time provided in Project SHE\n         Manual.\n\n\n3.3      Indian statutory requirements\n\n3.3.1     Primary statutory regulations\n\n\n3.3.1.1  Contractor shall develop thorough understanding about Building and Other\n        Construction Workers (Regulation of Employment and Conditions of Service) Act\n        1996, Central Rules 1998, the building & other construction workers welfare cess\n        act 1996 and  central welfare rules 1998, Building and Other Construction\n       Workers [RE&C] Karnataka  Rules,  2006,  Building and Other Construction\n       Workers Welfare Cess act 1996 and Central Rules 1998, Notification [Central &\n         State] – Collection of Cess, not only to satisfy the Inspectors’ perspective but the\n       use  of  legislation  as  the  strong  tool  for  effective SHE management  at\n        construction worksites. Contractor is strongly advised to practice the principle\n         of voluntary compliance.\n\n\n 3.3.1.2 In order to facilitate the contractor for better understanding on the various provisions of\n         the above Act and Rules, a tabulated information highlighting the Sections/Rules\n          referring to the corresponding registration of contractors, maintenance of registers and\n         records, hours of work and wages, cess & welfare, medical  facilities and safety\n        requirements are given in Appendix No. 2. It is an indicative one and not a limiting list.\n\n\n3.3.2   In addition, the construction works shall be undertaken in accordance with all applicable\n         legislation and Indian statutory requirements listed below but not limiting to:\n\n\n                i)     Indian Electricity Act 2003 and Rules 1956\n\n                  ii)    National Building Code, 2005\n\n                  iii)    Factories Act, 1948, Delhi, Uttarpradesh, Haryana Factories Rules.\n\n             iv)   Motor Vehicles Act as amended in 1994 and The Central Motor Vehicles Rules,\n              1989.\n\n           v)    Indian Road Congress Code IRC: SP: 55-2001 ‘Guidelines on Safety In Road\n               Construction Zones.\n\n             vi)   The Petroleum Act, 1934 and Rules 1976\n\n              vii)  Gas Cylinder Rules, 2003\n\n               viii)   Indian Explosives Act. 1884, along with the Explosives substance Act 1908 and\n                the Explosives Rules 1983\n\n             ix)   The (Indian) Boilers Act, 1923\n\n           x)   The Public Liability Insurance Act 1991 and Rules 1991\n\n             xi)   Minimum Wages Act, 1948 and Rules 1950"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 13,
>       "text": "xii)   Contract Labour Act, 1970 and Rules 1971\n\n                               xiii)   Child Labour (Prohibitions & Regulations) Act, 1986 and Rules 1950\n\n                        xiv)  Environment Protection Act, 1986 and Rules 1986\n\n                     xv)   Air (Prevention and control of Pollution) Act, 1981\n\n                        xvi)  Water (Prevention and Control of Pollution) Act, 1974\n\n                          xvii)  The Noise Pollution (Regulation & Control) Rules, 2000\n\n                           xviii)  Notification on Control of Noise from Diesel Generator (DG) sets, 2002\n\n                       xix)  Recycled Plastic Usage Rules, 1998\n\n                    xx)   Notification, Central Ground Water Board, Act January 1997\n\n                       xxi)  Manufacture, Storage & Import of Hazardous Chemicals Rules, 1989\n\n                          xxii)  The Hazardous Waste (Management & Handling) Rules, 1989\n\n                           xxiii) Hazardous Waste Management Rules 1989 (as amended in 1999)\n\n                      xxiv) Karnataka Preservation of Trees act, 1976 & rules, Batteries (Management and\n                         Handling) Rules\n\n                    xxv)  Fly ash utilization notification, Sept 1999 as amended in August 2003\n\n                      xxvi) Guidelines of Karnataka Urban Arts Commission\n\n                        xxvii) Mysore Tramway Act.\n\n\n          3.3.3    Workman Compensation Act, 1923 along with allied Rules\n\n\n           3.3.3.1  The contractor shall ensure that all his employees / workmen are covered under\n               ‘Workmen Compensation Act’ and shall pay compensation to his workmen as\n               and when the eventuality for the same arises.\n\n\n          3.3.4     Notwithstanding the above Act/Rules, there  is nothing  in those  to exempt the\n                     contractor from the purview of any other Act or Rule in Republic of India for the safety\n                      of men and materials.\n\n\n          3.3.5        If the requirements stated in this document are less stringent than or in conflict with\n                    the country’s applicable legislation, the latter shall apply.\n\n\n          3.4       International Standards, Guidelines & ISO Certifications\n\n\n          3.4.1    The works should be undertaken in accordance with the applicable international\n                     guidelines, standards and specifications on SHE and every contract shall aim to\n                   achieve ISO certifications listed below during the currency of the contract:\n\n\n             OHSAS 18001-1999   : Occupational Health and Safety Management System.\n\n                ISO 14001-2004         : Environmental Management Systems.\n\n          3.4.2    The process of certification shall start immediately after the award of the work and\n                  complete within reasonable time. Towards this, the contractor shall undertake the\n                    required steps including appointment of ISO consultant for obtaining the certification\n                on  Occupational  Health  and  Safety  Management  System  and  Environment\n                Management System.\n\n\n          3.4.3     In case of failure on the part of the contractor, the Employer at the cost of the\n                  contractor shall do the same."

>   "text": "Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\n                                                          240\n                i)    every compressed air system in a tunnel is provided with emergency power\n               supply for maintained continued supply of compressed air as per Rule 155 of\n         BOCWR\n\n                  ii)    watertight bulkhead doors are installed at the entrance of a tunnel to prevent\n                 flooding.\n\n                  iii)    reliable and effective means of communication such as telephone or walkie-\n                   talkie are provided and maintained for arranging better effective communication\n                 at an excavation or tunnelling work as per Rule 136 of BOCWR.\n\n             iv)     all portable electrical hand tools and inspection lamp used in under ground and\n               confined space at an excavation or tunnelling work is operated at a voltage not\n              exceeding 24V.\n\n           v)    only flame proof equipment of appropriate type as per IS:5571:2000 and or\n                other relevant national standard is used inside the tunnel\n\n             vi)    petrol or LPG of any other flammable substances are not used, stored inside the\n                tunnel except with prior approval from Employer, and also no oxy-acetylene gas\n                    is used in a compressed air environment in excavation or tunnelling\n\n              vii)   adequate number of water outlets provided for fire fighting purpose, an audible\n                     fire alarm and adequate number and types of fire extinguishers are provided\n             and maintained.\n\n               viii)  temperature in any working chamber in an excavation or tunnelling work where\n              workers employed does not exceed 29C as per Rule 165 of BOCWR.\n\n             ix)     all working areas in a free air tunnel are provided with ventilation system as\n              approved by the Director General and the fresh air supplied in such tunnel is not\n                less than 6 m3/ min for each worker employed in tunnel as per Rule 153 of\n          BOCWR.\n\n\n34.3     Warning signs and notices:\n\n\n34.3.1   The contractor shall ensure that\n\n                i)     suitable warning signs or notices, required for the safety of building workers\n                carrying out the work of an excavation or tunnelling, shall be displayed or\n               erected at conspicuous  places in Hindi and in a language understood by\n                majority of such building workers at such building such excavation or tunnelling\n              work\n\n                  ii)    such warning signs and notices with regard to compressed air working shall\n                include\n\n                a)    the danger involved in such compressed air work\n\n                b)     fire and explosion hazard\n\n                 c)    the emergency procedures for rescue from such danger or hazards.\n\n\n35.0    Work Permit system\n\n\n35.1    The Contractor shall develop a Work Permit system, which is a formal written system\n        used to control certain types of work that are potentially hazardous. A work permit is a\n         document, which specifies the work to be done, and the precautions to be taken. Work\n         Permits form an essential part of safe systems of work for many construction activities.\n        They allow work to start only after safe procedures have been defined and they\n          provide a clear record that all foreseeable hazards have been considered. Permits to\n        Work are usually required in high-risk areas as identified by the Risk Assessments.\n\n\n35.2    A permit  is needed when construction work can only be carried out   if normal\n         safeguards are dropped or when new hazards are introduced by the work. Examples\n           of high-risk activities include but are not limited to:\n\n\n                 i)     Entry into confined spaces\n                  ii)   Work in close proximity to overhead power lines and telecommunication cables.\n                  iii)   Hot work.\n             iv)   To dig—where underground services may be located.\n           v)   Work with heavy moving machinery.\n\n\nBangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.              September 2026     Section Page 52 of 114",

bulkhead doors are installed at the entrance of a tunnel to prevent \nflooding. \niii) \nreliable and effective means of communication such as telephone or walkie-\ntalkie are provided and maintained for arranging better effective communication \nat an excavation or tunnelling work as per Rule 136 of BOCWR. \niv) \nall portable electrical hand tools and inspection lamp used in under ground and \nconfined space at an excavation or tunnelling work is operated at a voltage not \nexceeding 24V. \nv) \nonly flame proof equipment of appropriate type as per IS:5571:2000 and or \nother relevant national standard is used inside the tunnel \nvi) \npetrol or LPG of any other flammable substances are not used, stored inside the \ntunnel except with prior approval from Employer, and also no oxy-acetylene gas \nis used in a compressed air environment in excavation or tunnelling \nvii) \nadequate number of water outlets provided for fire fighting purpose, an audible \nfire alarm and adequate number and types of fire extinguishers are provided \nand maintained. \nviii) \ntemperature in any working chamber in an excavation or tunnelling work where \nworkers employed does not exceed 29C as per Rule 165 of BOCWR. \nix) \nall working areas in a free air tunnel are provided with ventilation system as \napproved by the Director General and the fresh air supplied in such tunnel is not \nless than 6 m3/ min for each worker employed in tunnel as per Rule 153 of \nBOCWR. \n \n34.3 \nWarning signs and notices: \n \n34.3.1 \nThe contractor shall ensure that  \ni) \nsuitable warning signs or notices, required for the safety of building workers \ncarrying out the work of an excavation or tunnelling, shall be displayed or \nerected at conspicuous  places in Hindi and in a language understood by \nmajority of such building workers at such building such excavation or tunnelling \nwork \nii) \nsuch warning signs and notices with regard to compressed air working shall \ninclude \na) \nthe danger involved in such compressed air work \nb) \nfire and explosion hazard \nc) \nthe emergency procedures for rescue from such danger or hazards. \n \n35.0 \nWork Permit system \n \n35.1 \nThe Contractor shall develop a Work Permit system, which is a formal written system \nused to control certain types of work that are potentially hazardous. A work permit is a \ndocument, which specifies the work to be done, and the precautions to be taken. Work \nPermits form an essential part of safe systems of work for many construction activities. \nThey allow work to start only after safe procedures have been defined and they \nprovide a clear record that all foreseeable hazards have been considered. Permits to \nWork are usually required in high-risk areas as identified by the Risk Assessments. \n \n35.2  \nA permit is needed when construction work can only be carried out if normal \nsafeguards are dropped or when new hazards are introduced by the work. Examples \nof high-risk activities include but are not limited to: \n \ni) \nEntry into confined spaces \nii) \nWork in close proximity to overhead power lines and telecommunication cables. \niii) \nHot work. \niv) \nTo dig—where underground services may be located. \nv) \nWork with heavy moving machinery. \n"
>     },

### Jee37894331f7ed66

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part2#Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part II: Safety|Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part II: Safety]]

>   "text": "Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\n241\n                          vi)   Working on electrical equipment\n                             vii)  Work with radioactive isotopes.\n                               viii)  Heavy lifting operations and lifting operations closer to live power line\n\n\n          35.3    The permit-to-work system should be fully documented, laying down:\n\n\n                                 i)   How the system works;\n                                    ii)   The jobs it is to be used for;\n                                     iii)   The responsibilities and training of those involved; and\n                          iv)  How to check its operation;\n\n\n          35.4    A Work Permit authorisation form shall be completed with the maximum duration\n                    period not exceeding 12 hours.\n\n\n          35.5    A copy of each Permit To Work shall be displayed, during its validity, in a conspicuous\n                     location in close proximity to the actual works location to which it applies.\n\n\n          36.0      Traffic Management\n\n\n          36.1    The basic objective of the following guidelines  is to lay down procedures to be\n                  adopted by contractor to ensure the safe and efficient movement of traffic and also to\n                  ensure the safety of workmen at construction sites.\n\n\n          36.2       All construction workers should be provided with high visibility jackets with reflective\n                   tapes as most of viaduct /tunnelling/station and depot works or either above or under\n                      right-of-way. The conspicuity of workmen at  all times shall be increased so as to\n                     protect from speeding vehicular traffic.\n\n\n          36.3    The guiding principles to be adopted for safety in construction zone are to\n\n\n                                 i)    Warn the road user clearly and sufficiently in advance.\n\n                                    ii)    Provide safe and clearly marked lanes for guiding road users.\n\n                                     iii)    Provide safe and clearly marked buffer and work zones\n\n                          iv)   Provide adequate measures that control driver behaviour through construction\n                        zones.\n\n\n          36.4     Legal permission\n\n\n          36.4.1    In  all cases, the contractor shall employ proper precautions. Wherever operations\n                  undertaken are likely to interfere with public traffic, specific traffic management plans\n                      shall be drawn up and implemented by the contractor in consultation with the approval\n                      of local police authorities and/or the concerned metropolitan/civil authorities as the\n                 case may be.\n\n\n          36.4.2   Such  traffic management plans  shall  include  provision  for  traffic  diversion and\n                     selection of alternative routes for transport of equipment.  If necessary, the contractor\n                      shall carry out road widening before commencement of works to accommodate the\n                    extra load\n\n\n          36.5    The primary traffic control devices used in work zones shall include signs, delineators,\n                    barricades, cones, pylons, pavement markings and flashing lights.\n\n\n          36.6    The road construction and maintenance signs which fall into the same three major\n                    categories as do other traffic signs, that are Regulatory Signs, Warning Signs and\n                     Direction (or guidelines) Signs shall only be used. The IRC: 67 (Code of Practice for\n               Road Signs) provide a list of traffic signs. The size, colours and placement of sign\n                      shall confirm to IRC: 67.\n\n\n\n         Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.              September 2026     Section Page 53 of 114",

### J44c81c9cdd51865e

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Clause 42.0 — Medical Facilities|Clause 42.0 — Medical Facilities]]

> {
>   "clause_id": "42.0",
>   "fragments": [
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 64,
>       "text": "42.0     Medical Facilities\n\n\n42.1     Medical Examination\n\n\n42.1.1   The contractor shall arrange a medical examination of all his employees including his\n          sub-contractor employees employed as drivers, operators of  lifting appliances and\n          transport equipment before employing, after illness or injury,  if  it appears that the\n           illness or injury might have affected his fitness and, thereafter, once in every two years\n        up to the age of 40 and once in a year, thereafter.\n\n\n                i)    The Contractor shall maintain the confidential records of medical examination or\n                the physician authorized by the Employer.\n\n                  ii)   No building or other construction worker is charged for the medical examination\n             and the cost of such examination is borne by contractor employing such building\n               worker.\n\n                  iii)   The medical examination shall include: -\n\n\n                a)     Full medical and occupational history.\n\n                b)     Clinical examination with particular reference to\n\n                                    i)    General Physique;\n\n                                       ii)     Vision:- Total visual performance using standard orthorator  like\n                         Titmus  Vision  Tester  should be  estimated and  suitability  for\n                         placement  ascertained  in accordance  with  the  prescribed  job\n                           standards.\n\n                                        iii)    Hearing:  - Persons with normal must be able to hear a forced\n                          whisper at twenty-four feet. Persons using hearing aids must be\n                           able to hear a warning shout under noisy working conditions.\n\n                             iv)    Breathing:- Peak flow rate using standard peak flow meter and the\n                        average peak flow rate determined out of these readings of the test\n                          performed.  The  results  recorded  at  pre-placement  medical\n                          examination could be used as a standard for the same individual at\n                           the same altitude for reference during subsequent examination.\n\n                        v)   Upper Limbs: - Adequate arm function and grip\n\n                             vi)    Spine: - Adequately flexible for the job concerned.\n\n                               vii)   Lower Limbs: - Adequate leg and foot concerned.\n\n                                 viii)   General: - Mental alertness and stability with good eye, hand and\n                              foot coordination.\n\n                 c)   Any other tests which the examining doctor considers necessary\n\n\n42.1.2.     If the contractor fails to get the medical examination conducted as mentioned above,\n          the employer will have the right to get the same conducted by through an agency with\n           intimation to the contractor and deduct the cost and overhead charges."
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "text": "42.2     Occupational Health Centre\n\n          42.2.1   The contractor shall ensure at a construction site an occupational health centre,\n                 mobile or static is provided and maintained in good order. Services and facilities\n                as per the scale lay down in Schedule X of BOCWR. A construction medical\n                     officer appointed in an occupational health centre, possess the qualification as\n                     laid down in Schedule XI of BOCWR.\n\n\n          42.3    Ambulance van and room\n\n\n          42.3.1   The contractor  shall ensure  at a construction  site of a building or other\n                  construction work that an ambulance van and room are provided at such\n                  construction site or an arrangement is made with a nearby hospital for providing\n                such ambulance van for transportation of serious cases of accident or sickness\n                   of workers  to  hospital promptly and such ambulance van and room are\n                 maintained in good repair and is equipped with standard facilities specified in\n                Schedule IV and Schedule V of BOCWR.\n\n\n          42.4      First-aid boxes\n\n\n          42.4.1   The contractor shall ensure at a construction site one First-aid box for 100 workers\n                   provided and maintained for providing First-aid to the building workers. Every First-aid\n                 box  is  distinctly marked  “First-aid” and  is equipped with the  articles specified  in\n                 Schedule III of BOCWR.\n\n\n          42.5     HIV/ AIDS prevention and control\n\n\n          42.5.1   The contractor shall adopt the Employer’s Policy on “HIV  / AIDS Prevention and\n                    Control for Workmen Engaged by Contractors” and the copy of the policy is given in\n                Appendix No.: 4.\n\n          42.5.2   The Employer will engage a professional agency for implementing the guidelines laid\n               down in the policy and communicate to the contractor.\n\n          42.5.3   The Contractor shall extend necessary support to the appointed agency by deputing\n                    the workmen to attend the awareness creation programmes.\n\n          42.5.4   The contractor shall also extend necessary organizational support to the appointed\n                 agency  for the  effective implementation  of the Employers’ workplace  policy on\n                 HIV/AIDS for workmen of the Contractors.\n\n          42.5.5   As laid down in the policy the contractor shall identify peer educators (1 for every 100\n                   workers) and refer them for professional training to the Employers’ appointed agency\n                       for the purpose.\n\n          42.5.6   The peer educators on completion of the training shall serve as the focal point\n                    for any information, education and awareness campaign among the workmen\n                 throughout the contract period.\n\n          42.5.7   The peer educators will be paid a monthly honorarium as fixed by the Employer for\n                   rendering his services in addition to his regular duty.\n\n          42.5.8   The total number of peer educators (1 for 100 workers) shall always be maintained by\n                    the contractor.\n\n          42.5.9    In case if these peer educators leave the contractor by creating vacancy, then\n                  the contractor at his own expense train the new replacement peer educator from\n                  the Employers’ appointed agency for the purpose.\n          42.5.10    It is suggested to the contractor that due care should be taken to select the peer\n                   educators from among the group of workmen so that they remain with the contractor\n                   throughout the contract period.\n\n          42.6     Prevention of mosquito breeding"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "text": "42.6.1   Measures shall be taken to prevent mosquito breeding at site. The measures to be\n         taken shall include:\n\n\n                i)    Empty cans, oil drums, packing and other receptacles, which may retain water\n                  shall be deposited at a central collection point and shall be removed from the\n                   site regularly.\n\n                  ii)      Still waters shall be treated at least once every week with oil in order to prevent\n              mosquito breeding.\n\n                  iii)    Contractor’s equipment and other items on the site, which may retain water,\n                  shall be stored, covered or treated in such a manner that water could not be\n                 retained.\n\n             iv)   Water storage tanks shall be provided.\n\n\n42.6.2    Posters in both Hindi, English and local language which draw attention to the dangers\n           of permitting mosquito breeding, shall be displayed prominently on the site.\n\n\n42.6.3   The contractor at periodic interval shall arrange to prevent mosquito breeding\n        by fumigation  / spraying of insecticides. Most  effective insecticides  shall\n         include SOLFAC WP 10 or Baytex, The Ideal Larvicide etc.\n\n\n42.7     Alcohol and drugs\n\n\n42.7.1   The contractor shall ensure at  all times that no employee  is working under the\n          influence of alcohol / drugs which are punishable under Govt. regulations.\n\n\n42.7.2   Smoking  at  public worksites by any employee  is also prohibited as per Govt.\n          regulations."
>     }
